### Step 3a as built

**Built** in OA_NEW `3529f24` on `slice-08`, one commit, the 17 files of the Files table, after two step 3 commits the full suite asked for: `ca530b7` (`kernel/admission.py` read `market.sources.IST`, which `tests/unit/test_market_windows.py`'s layering guard forbids; it now keeps its own IST constant, and the guard is unchanged) and `829b038` (the missing `serve_kernel` `SIGTERM` case in `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, Linux only, and the statistics-through-admission row in `tests/unit/test_admission_checks.py`, where another strategy's admitted entry is the desk's open structure and a new entry is refused `ADM-R13` with `Desk already has an open option structure (1/1)`).

**Run.** Windows, Python 3.14 (`.venv-slice01`), through `scripts/safe_tests.py`: the completion conditions' files plus `tests/unit/test_market_windows.py`, `tests/integration/test_kernel_host.py`, `tests/unit/test_admission_checks.py` and `tests/contracts/test_operator_ui_routes.py`, twice in a row after the last source change: 813 passed and 12 skipped each time. Linux, on the EC2, once, in a throwaway folder with its own Python 3.13 venv built by `uv` from `requirements.lock` (removed afterwards): `tests/integration/test_unix_channel.py` and the two Linux-relevant `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py` cases, 37 passed, none skipped. The 22 break checks (`s11d_breaks.py` in the session scratchpad) each fail a listed test, and every file was restored (md5). `ast.parse` passes on all 16 edited Python files; `yaml.safe_load` on `resources/defaults.yaml`. Not run: the full suite.

**Differences from the text above**, each smaller than a decision:

| Text | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `_admit(…) -> tuple[Peer, dict] \| Refusal` | Returns the module's own `_Refused` (code and reason), as `ChannelCore` already raised internally | The refused reply needs the code and the label, not a ledger `Refusal` |
| `REVIEW_REPLY_TIMEOUT_S = MAX_REVIEW_SECONDS + REPLY_TIMEOUT_S` | `adapters/unix_channel.py` keeps its own `MAX_REVIEW_SECONDS = 300.0`, labelled as `cognition.review`'s; a test asserts 360.0 | The channel client loads no module outside `contracts` (`test_boundary`) |
| `KernelChannelClient` gains two keywords | Also four methods, `resolve_intent`, `review_binding`, `resolve`, `record_review`, one per new operation | One client call per method, as the six service operations |
| `MarketHost(…)` | Also `wait=` (a test seam for the main thread's wait) and `start_threads()`; the services are `bootstrap.serve_market`, `serve_strategy` and `serve_cognition`, reached from `run` for a production profile | Wiring; no new behaviour |
| `OpenAlgoTickSocket(…, connect=websocket.create_connection)` | `connect(url, timeout)`; `keepalive_secs` keyword (default 20); a `waits` list and `state()` for the heartbeat | Test seams and the heartbeat's `ticks` object |
| The review peers are the `strategy:` service peers | Filtered on `principal.value` | `Peer.principal` is a `Principal` |
| `ReviewChannelClient.request` raises on a reply other than `answered` | It raises `ChannelAmbiguous` with the outcome | The host's `_request_review` treats any exception as a failed request, as built |
| Row 17 mapped | `ticks.watch` also leaves `ROUTED`; `tests/integration/test_operator_routes.py`'s re-listing row 17 reads `mapped` | One kind per row |
| Tests: the watch's reconnect re-sends `authenticate` and both subscriptions | The watch group uses a stand-in socket; the re-send after a reconnect is proved in the socket group (`test_authenticate_resubscribe_and_reconnect`) | One owner per behaviour |
| Tests: unsealed windows `[09:31, 09:32]`, `next_wake` 09:31:07 | `[09:31, 09:32]` at `through` and `[09:32]` at the head; `next_wake` at 09:32:30 is 09:33:00 (the minute before 09:32's 09:33:07) | The text's two expectations used two different heads |
| Tests: the stop group through `serve` | The stop group calls `_stop_sequence` directly, with the socket connected and silent and a write blocked in `_HandOffStore`; the reference writer thread is a daemon | `serve`'s loop would run the queued write before stopping; a non-daemon writer hung pytest under the break check that leaves futures pending |
| Tests: the strategy host against a kernel subprocess | Linux: `ChannelKernel` and `ReviewChannelClient` over sockets served in the test process (the strategy's grant issued first, as `test_services` does); the cognition operations and the review core run on every platform through `handle` | `tests/conftest.py` has no kernel child; the in-process server exercises the same socket, peer and frame path |
| Tests: `serve_kernel`'s `SIGTERM` releases the lease `clean` | Exit 0 and the heartbeat `stopped` with `exit_code` 0; the lease is `None` or `clean` | The child binds no account, so it takes no lease |
| `tests/conftest.py` | `prod_profile` gains `market.ticks` (legacy's 10, 1, 30, 15, labelled synthetic) and `paths` (`run`, `evidence`) | The new required fields |
| EC2 runs through `scripts/safe_pytest.py` | `~/at-dev`'s runner imports legacy's `src.config` and runs `~/at-dev/.venv`; OA_NEW's own `scripts/safe_tests.py` refuses below 2012 MB available, and the host had 1.84–1.93 GB. The run used `systemd-run --user --scope` with `MemoryMax=1G`, no swap, `CPUQuota=50%`, `nice 19` and idle I/O, one run at a time | The coordinator's throwaway venv, with the legacy runner's limits |

**Left in OA_NEW, untracked:** the `build/` directory from step 2's first runs (unchanged; deleting it is the owner's call).

**Carried, unchanged:** every item under "Open, carried" above.

## Step 4 — the supervisor

<a id="step-4--the-supervisor-outline"></a>The authorities are the runtime spec's [supervisor](../../design/runtime-isolation-observability-deployment.md#the-supervisor-operationssupervisorpy), its [failure modes](../../design/runtime-isolation-observability-deployment.md#failure-modes-and-crash-points) F-H2 and F-S1…F-S4, PRF-OPS-02, PRF-OPS-03 and PRF-PRT-14, the slice's Fork call 4 (no watchdog), [step 1 as built](#step-1-as-built) (the `CFG-R07` blanking), [step 3](#step-3--the-kernel-host) and [its as-built record](#step-3-as-built) (the heartbeat, the identity record, exit codes 70, 71 and 72, and the carried `SIGSTOP` case), the owner's [standing rules](../../decisions/owner-answers-2026-10-27.md#standing-rules-2026-10-30), the coordinator's decisions of 3 November 2026 (exit 71 is reported, never acted on; legacy's monitor restart for the unit; step 1's blanking kept), and legacy's monitor at 445eb116 (`monitoring/service.py:369-450`, `monitoring/alert.py:2-17`, `:136-211`, `monitoring/sources.py:294-313`, `deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:1-26`). Step 4 is TASK-S11-03's supervisor part: PRF-OPS-02, PRF-OPS-03, PRF-PRT-14, and the `SIGSTOP` subprocess half of PRF-PRT-06 that step 3 carried here. It builds on OA_NEW `3529f24` or later; every OA_NEW citation below is at `3529f24`. **The supervisor holds no broker key, constructs no ledger writer, no coordinator, no order half and no channel command client, and nothing it does sends, settles, releases or grants.** No score, threshold or gate on money changes; every new timing value is a refusing setting.

**In plain words.** This step builds the watcher. Every few seconds (a setting, measured later) it opens the ledger read-only for one short, time-limited read, and copies out what is open: positions, their stops, commands waiting or at the broker, sessions, the kill switch and holds, and the kernel's recovery state. It then reads the kernel's "I am alive" file and asks systemd whether the kernel is running. Next it reads the broker's books through a read-only route, compares them with the ledger the same way the kernel does, and says for each stop whether the broker is holding it. From all that it gives one verdict per subject: the kernel is down, stalled, quiet, a send is overdue, the broker books are missing, or recovery is waiting for a person. It saves the whole assessment to its own folder first, then raises an alert, but only when a verdict changes, naming what the owner can do. It also watches free disk space, and when it falls below the reserve it stops the optional work (tests, research, backups' neighbours) and alerts; it deletes nothing. It never restarts or stops the kernel, never sends an order and cannot: it has no broker key and no sending code. When the kernel exits on a ledger problem (exit 71), the supervisor reports it and says that systemd restarts the kernel by itself after its lease runs out.

### Decisions

#### What legacy does here

* **Two monitors, one being retired.** `at-monitor.service` runs `monitoring.service` (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor.service:36`): a sweep every 90 s while the market is open and 600 s while it is shut (`monitoring/service.py:374-377`). Its successor `at-monitor-v2-sweep.service` runs `monitoring.v2_service` (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:11`), every 90 s open and shut (`monitoring/v2_service.py:185-186`, `:207`). At 445eb116, `deploy/monitor_v2_cutover.py:26-27`, `:117-157` disables `at-monitor.service` after the close once the successors are proven, and `deploy/session_reset.py:63-66` lists both as always on; which one ran on the host on a given day is **not checked** (deployment state). Both units restart `always` after 10 s.
* **The loop.** Each sweep's record is appended to `sweeps-<day>.jsonl` (`monitoring/service.py:101-105`) and handed to the alerter in a subprocess so an alerting fault cannot stop the sweep (`:108-119`). The loop never exits on a sweep's fault.
* **Alerts once per change.** `monitoring/alert.py:2-17`: an alert repeats only when the finding changes; state lives in `run/monitor/alert-state.json`, keyed by source, with a fingerprint of the findings (`:136-153`, `:155-211`), so a restart does not repeat a standing alert.
* **Reads.** The books come through `execution_client` with the shared trading key (`monitoring/sources.py:294-313`); the monitor holds the key and the order code.
* **The unit.** Always on, `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=10`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `Nice=19`, idle I/O, `MemoryHigh=768M`, `MemoryMax=1200M` (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:12-23`).
* **What it never did:** restart, stop or signal the trading processes.

#### The shared reads (`kernel/ledger/store.py`, `kernel/protection.py`, `kernel/lifecycle.py`)

The supervisor judges legs and commands with the kernel's own read functions, not copies of them (A03-F4: one read rule). Today they take the writer (`kernel/protection.py:273-372`, `kernel/lifecycle.py:229`) and use four of its members: `connection`, `rows`, `owned_orders` and `commands_with_prefix`, with `command`, `latest_attempt` and `attempts`. Only `commands_with_prefix`, `command`, `latest_attempt` and `attempts` are on the shared `_Reads` (`kernel/ledger/store.py:255-283`); `LedgerReader`'s versions open their own transaction each (`_bounded`, `:874-883`), which the snapshot's one transaction cannot nest.

* **`_Reads` gains** `connection`, a plain instance attribute that each class's `__init__` sets with `conn` (the writer already does, `kernel/ledger/store.py:289`; `LedgerReader.__init__` and `SnapshotReads.__init__` gain the same line; never a property, which would break the writer's assignment), `rows(table, where, args)` (the writer's unbounded select, `:440-442`, `:773-776`, moved up, refusing a non-index table as `LedgerReader.rows` does) and `owned_orders(account)` (moved from `LedgerWriter`, `:401-413`, body unchanged). `LedgerWriter` keeps every name and behaviour; `LedgerReader.rows` stays `_bounded` for the operator.
* **`SnapshotReads(_Reads)`** (new, `kernel/ledger/store.py`): `_Reads` over a connection already inside a transaction, adding nothing. Only `LedgerReader.snapshot` constructs it.
* **`LedgerReader.open(path, *, busy_timeout_ms: int = 5000)`** passes the timeout to `runtime.connect` (`kernel/ledger/runtime.py:71`, `:84`); existing callers keep 5000. The supervisor opens with `busy_timeout_ms = supervisor.read_budget_ms`, because the progress handler does not interrupt a busy wait, so the budget bounds the wait only through this timeout.
* **`LedgerReader.snapshot(budget_ms: int, read: Callable[[SnapshotReads], T]) -> T`** (a ledger-spec module change, reported to it): `BEGIN`; the first statement `SELECT sequence, event_hash FROM ledger_event ORDER BY sequence DESC LIMIT 1` fixes the read snapshot; `schema.check(conn)` (a refusal raises `SnapshotGap("schema", refusal.code)`); an apsw progress handler, every 1000 virtual-machine steps, interrupts once `time.monotonic()` passes the start plus `budget_ms`; then `read(SnapshotReads(conn))`; `COMMIT` and the handler removed in a `finally`. `apsw.InterruptError` becomes `SnapshotGap("budget", "<n> ms")`, `apsw.BusyError` `SnapshotGap("busy", …)`; any other `apsw.Error` propagates. The head is returned with `read`'s value as `(head, value)`.
* **`leg_view` splits in two,** with the same result: `leg_ledger(reads, position_id, role, *, exit_open=False, basket=None) -> LegLedger` is lines `:282-343` and `:350-363` (everything that reads the ledger), and `attach(ledger: LegLedger, observation, now, *, max_age_secs) -> LegView` is lines `:344-349` and `:364-372` (the live order's row in a fresh observation, then the ended reason). `LegLedger` carries every value `attach` reads: the `done` list, `filled`, `ledger_ended`, `exit_open` and `is_basket` (`basket is not None`), because `:364-368` and `_ended_reason` (`:375-388`) read `exit_open` and the basket flag after the observation is known. `leg_view(writer, …)` becomes `attach(leg_ledger(writer, …), …)`; every kernel caller stays unchanged. `_ended_reason` loses its unused `writer` argument. `_admitted` and `_completed` read through `reads.connection`, as now.
* **`ledger_view(reads, account)`** reads only `_Reads` members after the change; its signature's first argument is renamed `reads`. Its callers pass the writer, unchanged.

#### The read model (`operations/supervisor.py`)

**`read_model(reads: SnapshotReads, *, accounts, now) -> ReadModel`**, run inside `snapshot`, so every row is at one head. All of it is ledger only; no observation is read inside the transaction:

| Part | Rows |
|---|---|
| `sessions` | `session` rows with `state = 'OPEN'` |
| `positions` | `position` rows with `state != 'closed'`, each with its `protection_state` row |
| `legs` | Per position: `{"stop": leg_ledger(reads, pid, "stop", exit_open=…)}`; for a basket entry (its admitted `plan_ref` has `protective_legs`), one `leg_ledger` per protective role with the basket mapping built from the entry's command row and latest attempt exactly as `ProtectionLoop._evaluate_basket` builds it (`kernel/protection.py:484-503`). `exit_open` is `protection_state.exit_reason IS NOT NULL` |
| `commands` | `command_identity` rows in `ADMITTED`, `IN_FLIGHT`, `UNCERTAIN` or `WORKING` (admission's `_ACTIVE`, `kernel/admission.py:364`), with `age_s` = `now` − the `event_time` of the row's first event |
| `unresolved_attempts` | `command_attempt` rows in `CLAIMED` or `AMBIGUOUS` (`kernel/lifecycle.py:30-34`), with `age_s` from `claimed_at` |
| `held_reservations` | `reservation` rows with `state = 'HELD'` |
| `safety` | Every `safety_state` row (kill, holds, latch, check states) |
| `recovery` | The latest `RECOVERY_STAGE` payload (`kernel/recovery.py:424-427`) and the latest `RELEASE_BOUND`'s binding ID |
| `views` | `ledger_view(reads, account)` for each bound account (`accounts`) |

#### The cycle (`Supervisor.cycle() -> Assessment`)

`Supervisor(reader_path, *, observations: ObservationPort | None, store: EvidenceStore | None, index_dir: Path | None, alerts: AlertSink, state_path: Path | None, unit: UnitProbe, heartbeat: Path | None, identity: Path | None, reserve: ReserveCheck | None, policy: ReconciliationPolicy, settings, clock)`; `cycle()` runs, in order, each stage contained (a non-`KeyboardInterrupt` exception is a coverage gap `fault <stage> <TypeName>` and the cycle goes on, as legacy's sweep never stopped):

1. **Ledger.** `LedgerReader.open(reader_path)` (read-only, `kernel/ledger/runtime.py:71-96`), `snapshot(settings.supervisor_read_budget_ms, read_model)`, close. A `SnapshotGap`, a missing file (`missing`), a schema refusal at open (`schema`) or an `apsw.BusyError` at open (`busy`) is the coverage gap `ledger_gap <reason>` and the read model is `None`; nothing below guesses past it (F-S1, F-S2).
2. **Liveness.** The heartbeat JSON (`<paths.run>/health/kernel.json`, step 3), its age from `pass_ended_utc` against `clock.now()`; the kernel's identity record (`<paths.run>/identity/kernel.json`); `unit.show("at-kernel.service") -> UnitState(active_state, sub_state, main_pid) | None`. `SystemctlProbe` runs `systemctl show <unit> --property=ActiveState,SubState,MainPID` with a 5 s timeout; any failure is `None` and the gap `unit_unprobed`. An unreadable heartbeat is the gap `heartbeat_unreadable`.
3. **Observations.** For each bound account, in sorted order: `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positions` and `funds` through `observations`, one call at a time, never more than `supervisor.max_requests_per_minute` in any 60 s (a call that would exceed it is skipped and the account's books are the gap `observation_gap budget`). Each response's account, endpoint and domain are checked (ATA-06); a wrong account or endpoint is the gap `observation_gap mismatch`. `observations` `None` (no route composed) is `observation_gap route_unbound` for every account. The four make an `AccountObservation` with the observation loop's digest rule (`kernel/reconciliation.py:865-866`); the calls are not one atomic snapshot (`README.md:922`).
4. **Assessment.** For each account with a read model and observations: `kernel.reconciliation.assess(observation, model.views[account], policy, now)` for the orderbook and the tradebook only, as the kernel's loop reconciles only those two (`kernel/reconciliation.py:877-880`; `assess` returns at once for `positionbook` and `funds`, `:279-280`); the buckets (matched, conflicting, unattributed, unknown, gaps) go into the assessment as their counts and their findings' `(kind, command_key, leg_role, reason)`. An analyzer-domain observation against a live obligation stays `unknown`, as `assess` decides.
5. **Protection status.** For each position's legs: `attach(ledger, account_observation, now, max_age_secs=settings.max_observation_age_secs)`, then `classify(leg, "stop", now, max_age_secs=…)` for the stop role (a basket's stop included). Each open position also gets `target_exit` and `delta_exit`, which `classify` makes `runtime_dependent`. A basket's `target` role is listed with its leg state and no classification (`classify` has no kind for it). **Unobserved:** while the account has `observation_gap` this cycle, every stop of that account is recorded `unobserved` without calling `classify`, and no per-leg alert is raised for it; the account's one `observation_gap` alert says why (`classify` would read every stop `uncertain`, `kernel/protection.py:442-445`). **Not running:** when the kernel's verdict is `kernel_down` or `kernel_stalled`, or the heartbeat's `stage` is not `OPERATING`, every `runtime_dependent` entry reads `runtime_dependent (not running)` (A03-F5).
6. **Verdicts** (below), from the read model, liveness and observations.
7. **Publish, then alert.** The assessment's content (below) is published to `store` (an `EvidenceStore` over `supervisor.assessment_path`, `kernel/ledger/evidence_store.py:86-135`) **only when it differs from the last published content with `at` and every age field removed** (`heartbeat.age_s`, the `age_s` of commands and attempts); then one line `{"at", "digest", "head", "verdicts"}` is appended to `index_dir/<YYYY-MM-DD IST>.jsonl`. An unchanged cycle writes nothing, so a quiet desk adds nothing. **Retention:** none in the first release, as the runtime spec sets for every required-evidence store; the growth is one object per change, and `supervisor.assessment_path` must be on a different filesystem from `ledger.path` (below), so it cannot fill the ledger's disk. An `OSError` from either is the counted gap `assessment_unpublished` (the counter is in the next assessment); the alerts still run (Combined-F2; PRF-OPS-05's row). Then the alerts (below).
8. **Reserve.** `reserve.check()` (below), after publication, so its alert and its stop are recorded in the next assessment.

#### Verdicts

**Demand** is any open position, command in `commands`, unresolved attempt or open session in the read model; with no read model, demand is unknown and treated as present. One verdict for the kernel, and per-subject verdicts after it:

| Verdict | Subject | When | The owner's options, in the alert |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kernel_down` | `kernel` | `UnitState.active_state` is not `active`, or `main_pid` is 0 | Per the heartbeat's `exit_code`: **71** "The kernel stopped on its ledger. systemd starts it again after 60 s; it waits for its send lease to expire and resumes by itself (Fork call J). The broker-held stop covers the gap. If the next assessments still show it down, read the kernel's journal and the ledger disk"; **72** "Another holder took the send lease. Find the other kernel; do not start a second one"; **70** "The kernel crashed; systemd restarts it after 60 s, at most 3 times in 15 minutes"; otherwise "The kernel is not running. `systemctl status at-kernel`" |
| `kernel_starting` | `kernel` | The unit is `active` and its `main_pid` differs from the heartbeat's `pid` (or there is no heartbeat): a new process that has not finished a pass, such as a restart waiting for its lease (step 3's `wait_for_lease` writes no heartbeat) | "The kernel restarted (pid <main_pid>) and has not completed a pass yet; after a crash it waits for its send lease, then resumes by itself (Fork call J). The broker-held stop covers the gap" |
| `kernel_stalled` | `kernel` | The unit is `active`, its `main_pid` equals the heartbeat's `pid`, the heartbeat's age is above `supervisor.stall_secs`, and there is demand. Never given while the pids differ | "The kernel is running but has not completed a pass for <age> s. Stops resting at the broker hold; trails and tick exits are not running. No watchdog restarts it (Fork call 4): restart it by hand, or square off through the broker" |
| `kernel_quiet` | `kernel` | As `kernel_stalled`, with no demand | None: never alerted |
| `kernel_ok` | `kernel` | Otherwise, with a readable heartbeat; with no unit state and no heartbeat, `kernel_unknown` | — |
| `dispatch_overdue` | `dispatch:<command_key>#<attempt_no>` | A heartbeat `in_flight` entry with `over_deadline` true (F-D6, `kernel/coordinator.py:65-70`) | "A broker call is past its deadline; the adapter's total deadline makes it ambiguous, and reconciliation settles it" |
| `observation_gap` | `observation:<account wire>` | The supervisor's books for the account are missing, incomplete or older than `reconciliation.max_observation_age_secs` (`kernel/admission.py:250-261`), or the heartbeat's `observation_health` for it shows no `last_as_of` within that age | "The broker's books are not being read for this account; stops cannot be confirmed" |
| `recovery_declined` | `recovery` | The latest `RECOVERY_STAGE` has `to` null and a non-empty `declined` | "Recovery is waiting for a person: `at recovery advance` after checking the declined items <list>" |
| `ledger_gap` | `ledger` | Step 1's gap | "The supervisor could not read the ledger: <reason>" |
| Classification change | `leg:<position_id>:<role>` | A leg's classification differs from the last cycle's | "<role> of <position> is now <classification>" |

**Exit 71 is reported, never acted on** (the coordinator's decision): the supervisor has no right to start, stop or signal `at-kernel.service` (its polkit rule names only `at.slice`), and the restart is systemd's (Fork call J).

#### Alerts once per change (`AlertSink`, `JournalAlerts`)

* **State** `{subject: fingerprint}` is kept in `state_path` (`<paths.run>/supervisor/alert-state.json`), written atomically after each cycle's alerts, read at start, so a restart repeats no standing alert, as legacy's (`monitoring/alert.py:155-211`). An unreadable state file is an empty state and one WARNING.
* **Rule.** For each subject with a verdict or classification this cycle, an alert when its fingerprint (the verdict with its reason, or the classification) differs from the stored one; for each stored subject absent this cycle, one `cleared <subject>` alert. `kernel_ok`, `kernel_quiet` and `kernel_unknown` are fingerprints but raise no alert except `cleared` of a previous alerting verdict.
* **`AlertSink.raise_(subject, text)`.** Production composes `JournalAlerts`: one `logging` record at WARNING on `autonomous_trading.supervisor.alert`, `ALERT <subject>: <text>`. Tests compose a recording sink. SLICE-10's operator route replaces it when composed; the assessment store already holds every alert's text.

#### The assessment's content

Canonical JSON: `{"format": "at-assessment/1", "at": clock.now().utc_iso, "head": [sequence, event_hash] | null, "ledger_gap": reason | null, "heartbeat": {state, pid, stage, pass_seq, pass_ended_utc, age_s, exit_code} | null, "unit": {active_state, sub_state, main_pid} | null, "identity": {settings_revision, bundle_digest} | null, "observations": {account: {endpoint: as_of utc_iso}}, "assessment": {account: {endpoint: {matched, conflicting, unattributed, unknown, gaps, findings}}}, "legs": [{position_id, role, state, classification}], "verdicts": [{subject, verdict, reason}], "demand": true | false | null, "gaps": [...], "unpublished": n, "reserve": {state, root, free_bytes} | null, "alerts": [{subject, text}], "settings": {the supervisor's reconciliation values}}`. When the kernel identity record's settings show different reconciliation values from the supervisor's, the gap `settings_differ <key>` is added: the two judge freshness by different rules.

#### The reserve (`operations/isolation.py`, its first part)

* **`check_reserve(roots: Mapping[str, Path], reserve_bytes: int | None, *, usage=shutil.disk_usage) -> ReserveState`**: `unbound` when `reserve_bytes` is `None`; else the root with the least free space and `crossed` when that is below `reserve_bytes`, `ok` otherwise; an `OSError` on a root is `unreadable <root>`. The roots are the parents of `ledger.path`, `paths.evidence`, `market.store_path` and `supervisor.assessment_path`, those bound.
* **`ReserveCheck`** holds the last state and, on the change into `crossed` only, calls `stop_optional()`, which runs `systemctl stop at.slice` (the system manager; 30 s timeout) and records its exit status; then the alert `reserve_crossed <root> <free_bytes>` with "Optional work (tests, research) is stopped. Nothing was deleted. Free space by hand." Leaving `crossed` alerts `cleared reserve`; nothing restarts `at.slice`. `unbound` is one gap `reserve_unbound` per cycle and no stop (`storage.critical_reserve_bytes` is refusing, measured in step 9). Nothing is ever deleted (A09-F3). Step 6 adds `admit` and the envelope to this module.
* **`polkit_rule(user: str, unit: str) -> str`**: the rule text granting `user` the action `org.freedesktop.systemd1.manage-units` only when `action.lookup("unit") == unit` and `action.lookup("verb") == "stop"`, everything else `polkit.Result.NOT_HANDLED`. Step 7 renders it for `at-supervisor` and `at.slice` and writes it in the root step; the unit's `NoNewPrivileges` rules out sudo.
* **No trading unit is under `at.slice`.** systemd nests dash-named slices, so `at-<x>.slice` is a child of `at.slice` (legacy has `at-research.slice`). The kernel, market, strategy, operator and supervisor units never carry a `Slice=` that is `at.slice` or a name starting `at-` and ending `.slice`; only optional work and the backup do (the runtime spec's envelopes). Stopping `at.slice` therefore cannot stop trading. **Carried to step 7:** a render check that refuses any such `Slice=` on those units (`tests/contracts/test_rendered_units.py`).

#### Reader impact (`operations/verification.py`)

**`measure_supervisor_impact(path, *, entries, cycles_per_entry, budget_ms, clock) -> dict`** extends `measure_reader_impact` (`operations/verification.py:37-87`): the same composition commits `entries` entries; between commits, `cycles_per_entry` supervisor ledger reads (`LedgerReader.open`, `snapshot(budget_ms, read_model)`, close) run, and after each commit the writer runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)`. Returned for the run with the reads and a run without: `wal_max_bytes`, `checkpoints` (count, and how many returned `busy` 0 with `log == checkpointed`), `commit_ms` (`count, p50, p95, max`). It sets no limit; step 9 makes it a `Measurement`.

#### The composition and the start (`operations/supervisor.py`, `bootstrap.py`)

`bootstrap.py` imports `SimulatedBroker`, `Coordinator`, `LedgerWriter` and `Recovery` at load time (`bootstrap.py:16-29`), so the supervisor's process must not load it. **`compose_supervisor` and `serve_supervisor` live in `operations/supervisor.py`, with `main()`**, and `pyproject.toml`'s `at-supervisor` entrypoint points at `autonomous_trading.operations.supervisor:main`. `main` reuses the start steps through narrow imports only: `settings.loader.effective` (the resolver), the blanking, `bootstrap`'s release and start helpers moved into **`operations/deployment.py`** (README §6, `README.md:1065`: "Record staged release transitions and compatibility"), which imports no kernel or adapter module at load time: the manifest functions (`bootstrap.py:555-860`: `load_manifest`, `verify_loaded`, `bundle_digest`, `compatible` and their helpers), `required_fields` with `_PRODUCTION_ADDS` and `REFUSES_ON_ARTIFACT`, `_mask`, `_write_identity` and `_install_logging`. `bootstrap` imports each name back, so every existing caller and test keeps working unchanged. `bootstrap.main_supervisor` stays as a thin alias to the new `main`.

* **`compose_supervisor(eff, *, observations=None, alerts=None, unit=None, clock=None) -> Supervisor`**: `LiveClock` by default; the `EvidenceStore` over `supervisor.assessment_path`; the index directory `<supervisor.assessment_path>/index`; the state path; `SystemctlProbe()` by default; `ReconciliationPolicy` from the supervisor's reconciliation settings, as the kernel builds it (`bootstrap.py:256-258`); `ReserveCheck` over the roots above. It imports and constructs no `LedgerWriter`, `Coordinator`, `KernelHost`, recovery, OpenAlgo adapter, simulated broker or channel client. **`observations` stays `None` in production in this step:** the read-only route is the native read-only adapter (TASK-S07-06) or step 5's mediator, and neither exists yet, so every production cycle records `observation_gap route_unbound` and still reports the ledger, liveness and the reserve.
* **`serve_supervisor(eff, environ, *, digest_) -> int`**: `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` set a `threading.Event`; `while not stop: sup.cycle(); stop.wait(settings.supervisor_poll_secs)`; returns 0. A cycle never raises out (each stage is contained); an exception outside `cycle` exits 70 and systemd restarts it (step 7: `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=10`, legacy's monitor).
* **`main()`** runs `--check` through `bootstrap.check` (the `--check` path may load `bootstrap`; it composes nothing and runs no loop); otherwise it resolves, blanks, verifies the manifest (refusing on a mismatch, `REFUSES_ON_ARTIFACT`), checks the required fields and the filesystem rule, writes the identity record and, with a production profile, calls `serve_supervisor`; a test or simulation profile still exits 78. `bootstrap.run("supervisor", …)` calls the same `main` body. Before composing, each name `_mask` blanked (`eff.masked`, `bootstrap.py:1098-1102`) is raised once as the alert `credential_blanked <NAME>` through the composed sink and recorded in the first assessment's `gaps`.
* **`required_fields("supervisor", "production")`** adds `ledger.path`, `paths.run`, `accounts`, `supervisor.assessment_path` and the five reconciliation fields (`_PRODUCTION_ADDS`).

#### Settings (`settings/schema.py`)

* **New field `supervisor.assessment_path`**, kind `("path", "required_evidence")`, scope `("supervisor", "backup")`, environment `AT_SUPERVISOR_ASSESSMENT_PATH`, register `deployment`, shipping `null`. **Its filesystem:** the supervisor's start refuses (`refused: supervisor.assessment_path shares a filesystem with ledger.path`) when the existing nearest ancestor of each path has the same `st_dev`; the filesystems themselves are chosen when step 7 creates the directories, so step 7's `check` repeats the rule on the host.
* **Five fields widen their scope** to a new `shared:reconciliation` = `("kernel", "supervisor")`: `reconciliation.max_observation_age_secs`, `.clock_skew_secs`, `.working_escalation_secs`, `.foreign_response`, `.unknown_domain_settlement` (`settings/schema.py:215-241`). No value, default or register changes; the supervisor reads the same values to judge freshness and matching by the kernel's rule.

#### Fork calls in this step

* **S. The supervisor's unit restarts as legacy's monitor:** `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=10`, the same in both monitor units (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:13-14`; `deploy/systemd/at-monitor.service:37-38`), rendered in step 7. It touches no money; recorded because the owner chose it.
* **Fork call 4 is kept:** no watchdog; a frozen kernel is `kernel_stalled`, alerted once, and a human acts. The supervisor restarts, stops or signals no trading process, as legacy's monitor never did; this includes exit 71, which systemd and the lease wait handle (Fork call J).

#### Deviations in this step

* **Against PRF-PRT-14's "refuses to start with any broker credential name in its environment" (`CFG-R07`):** the start is not refused. Step 1's resolver blanks every forbidden name before composing (`settings/loader.py:449-454`; `bootstrap.py:1098-1102`) and the supervisor raises `credential_blanked <NAME>` and records it; legacy never refused a start on this, and the blanked value is gone before any code reads it (the coordinator's decision of 3 November 2026). Reported to the protection spec.
* **One cadence, as the successor monitor:** `supervisor.poll_secs` in and out of session, as `monitoring.v2_service` swept every 90 s both open and shut (`monitoring/v2_service.py:185-186`); the older `monitoring.service`, which the cutover retires (`deploy/monitor_v2_cutover.py:26-27`), slept 600 s while shut (`monitoring/service.py:374-377`). Step 9 measures the value; 90 s is evidence.
* **Four reads, not three:** the supervisor also reads `funds`, because the shared leg read takes the kernel's whole-observation freshness rule (`kernel/admission.py:250-261`). Reported to the runtime spec.
* **The reads are shared, not copied:** `leg_view` splits into `leg_ledger` and `attach`, and `ledger_view` reads only `_Reads`; a protection-spec and lifecycle-spec module change with no change in result, reported to both.
* **Alerts go to the journal** until SLICE-10's operator route is composed; legacy also wrote to the journal and Telegram (`monitoring/alert.py:82-133`).
* **The basket's `target` leg is listed, not classified;** unfilled basket entries appear as commands, not legs.

#### Carried to later steps

* **Step 5 or TASK-S07-06:** the production `observations` route; until then `observation_gap route_unbound`.
* **Step 6:** `admit` and the envelope in `operations/isolation.py`; `telemetry=`.
* **Step 7:** `at-supervisor.service` (`Restart=always`, `RestartSec=10`, `NoNewPrivileges`, `Nice=19`, idle I/O, soft caps from `envelope.supervisor.*`, `OOMScoreAdjust=500`); the polkit rule; read access for the supervisor's user to the kernel's `health/` and `identity/` files and the ledger's `-wal` and `-shm` through `at-ledger-readers`; PRF-PRT-14's Linux half (the unit, under its own user, cannot read the kernel's credential or environment), proved with PRF-OPS-07; the render check that no kernel, market, strategy, operator or supervisor unit has a `Slice=` of `at.slice` or an `at-*.slice`; `check` repeating the rule that `supervisor.assessment_path` and `ledger.path` are on different filesystems; a real `systemctl show` against the rehearsal's units.
* **Step 9:** `supervisor.poll_secs`, `read_budget_ms`, `stall_secs`, `max_requests_per_minute` and `storage.critical_reserve_bytes` measured; `measure_supervisor_impact` as a `Measurement`.
* **SLICE-10's operator route** for the alerts, and the last assessment's age in the operator interface (F-S4).

### Tests

Every value is synthetic. `RecordedClock`, `build` and the ledger fixtures are `tests/conftest.py`'s. `Books(broker)` (new in the test file) wraps a `SimulatedBroker` and exposes only `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positions` and `funds`. `Probe(state)` returns a fixed `UnitState`. `Sink` records `(subject, text)`. Settings: `poll_secs` 5, `read_budget_ms` 200, `stall_secs` 60, `max_requests_per_minute` 12, `max_observation_age_secs` 60.

**`tests/integration/test_supervisor.py` (T-76), PRF-OPS-02 and PRF-OPS-03:**

| Group | Cases |
|---|---|
| One transaction | Over `build` with an open position: a `read` callable that commits a new entry through the writer half-way (on another connection) → the read model's head and rows are those before it, and the next cycle's head is after it. `snapshot` calls `read` once, and the connection is out of its transaction when `snapshot` returns and when `read` raises. A spy on `Books` records that every call starts after `snapshot` returned |
| Ledger gaps (F-S1, F-S2) | `read_budget_ms` 1 with `read` spinning a query over a 50,000-row synthetic table → `ledger_gap budget`, no verdict built from rows, the cycle still publishes. **Busy:** a second connection runs `PRAGMA locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE`, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and one insert into a scratch table, and holds the transaction (in WAL mode an exclusive-locking writer keeps the shared memory, so a new reader waits); the supervisor opens with `busy_timeout_ms` 50 → `ledger_gap busy` within 3.5 s (the 5 s default would exceed it), whether the `BusyError` comes at open or inside the snapshot; a ledger whose schema version is one past the supported one → `ledger_gap schema`; no file → `ledger_gap missing` |
| Shared reads | **Reference, not the new code:** the test file keeps a verbatim copy of `leg_view`'s body at `3529f24` (`kernel/protection.py:273-372`) as `reference_leg_view(writer, …)`. Over the scenarios built by `tests/unit/test_protection_states.py`'s ledger fixtures and by `tests/unit/test_protection_basket.py`'s own `leg_view` helper (its line 94): for each position, role, observation (fresh, stale, none) and `exit_open` value, `attach(leg_ledger(reader_reads, …), obs, now, …) == reference_leg_view(writer, …)` and `leg_view(writer, …) == reference_leg_view(writer, …)`; `ledger_view(reader_reads, account) == ledger_view(writer, account)`. Two fixed cases with hand-written expected `LegView` values: a basket stop with a placed level and no completion (`confirmed_level` the placed level), and a stop ended by a human cancel with `exit_open` true (`ended_reason` `human:<key>`) |
| Assessment buckets | Books equal to the ledger → every order matched; a resting stop missing from the orderbook → conflicting with the kernel's reason; an order the ledger does not know → unattributed; a partial orderbook (`coverage.complete == "no"`) → gap; an analyzer-domain orderbook against a live entry → `unknown`, never matched; a funds response for another account → `observation_gap mismatch`; `assess` called for `orderbook` and `tradebook` only (a spy), never `positionbook` or `funds` |
| Classification | Stop resting at its confirmed level, books 30 s old → `broker_held`; 61 s old → `unobserved` (the account has `observation_gap`) and no leg alert; trigger differs by 0.05 with fresh books → `uncertain` and one leg alert; `observations` `None` → every stop `unobserved`, one `observation_gap` alert per account and no leg alert; `target_exit` and `delta_exit` → `runtime_dependent`; the same with `Probe` inactive → `runtime_dependent (not running)` on both while the stop stays `broker_held` |
| Verdicts (independent expectations) | Heartbeat 30 s old, demand → `kernel_ok`; 61 s, demand → `kernel_stalled`; 61 s, no demand → `kernel_quiet` and no alert; `Probe` `inactive` with heartbeat `failed`, `exit_code` 71 → `kernel_down` whose alert text contains "resumes by itself" and "Fork call J"; 72 and 70 each their text; `in_flight` with `over_deadline` → `dispatch_overdue dispatch:<key>#1`; latest `RECOVERY_STAGE` `to` null, `declined` `[6]` → `recovery_declined`; no `Probe` answer and no heartbeat → `kernel_unknown` and gaps `unit_unprobed`, `heartbeat_unreadable`; `Probe` active with `main_pid` 4242 and the heartbeat's `pid` 4100, 300 s old, demand → `kernel_starting` with the lease-wait text, never `kernel_stalled`; the same with no heartbeat → `kernel_starting` |
| Never acts | Over every verdict case and the reserve group: `subprocess.run`, `subprocess.Popen`, `subprocess.call`, `subprocess.check_call`, `subprocess.check_output`, `os.kill`, `os.killpg` and `os.system` patched to record → the only recorded calls are `systemctl show at-kernel.service …` and, in the reserve group, `systemctl stop at.slice`; nothing else is called |
| Alerts once per change | Five cycles of `kernel_stalled` → one alert; then `kernel_ok` → one `cleared kernel`; a new `Supervisor` over the same `state_path` with `kernel_stalled` standing → no alert; an unreadable state file → one alert and one WARNING |
| Publication | The index line's `digest` resolves through `EvidenceStore.resolve` to the content; publish before index (the store's object exists when the index write starts, by a patched index writer); three cycles with nothing changed but the clock → one object and one index line; a verdict change → a second of each; `store` root a file → `assessment_unpublished` gap, `unpublished` 1 in the next assessment, the alert still raised. **Filesystem rule:** `st_dev` patched equal for the two paths → the start refuses with `supervisor.assessment_path shares a filesystem with ledger.path`; different → it proceeds |
| Reserve (PRF-OPS-05's reserve row) | `usage` patched: free above the reserve → `ok`, no stop; below → one `systemctl stop at.slice`, alert `reserve_crossed`, no file under any root removed (listing before and after equal); still below next cycle → no second stop; above → `cleared reserve`; `reserve_bytes` `None` → gap `reserve_unbound`, no stop. `polkit_rule("at-supervisor", "at.slice")` contains the user, the unit, the verb `stop` and `NOT_HANDLED`, and no other unit name |
| Rate | Two accounts, `max_requests_per_minute` 6, one cycle → 6 calls, the second account's books `observation_gap budget` |
| Settings drift | Kernel identity record with `clock_skew_secs` 2 and the supervisor's 1 → gap `settings_differ reconciliation.clock_skew_secs` |
| Containment | `assess` patched to raise `ValueError` → gap `fault assessment ValueError`, publication and alerts still run |
| Reader impact (A03-F4) | `measure_supervisor_impact(tmp ledger, entries=20, cycles_per_entry=3, budget_ms=200)`: every checkpoint in both runs returns `busy` 0 with `log == checkpointed`; `wal_max_bytes` and `commit_ms` present for both runs |
| Frozen kernel (PRF-OPS-03, PRF-PRT-06's frozen half; Linux only, else `skipped: needs SIGSTOP`) | `kernel_child(...)` (new in `tests/conftest.py`: `python -I -c` composing `compose_simulation` over a ledger under `tmp_path`, a filled entry with its broker stop confirmed, `KernelHost(...).serve()` writing its heartbeat). Control: running, `Probe` active with its pid → `kernel_ok`; a target tick written to its market store → `EXIT_STARTED` within two passes. Then `SIGSTOP`, the tick written again on a second position: for 45 s the heartbeat's `pass_seq` and the ledger head do not change and no `EXIT_STARTED` is recorded; the supervisor, `stall_secs` 30, gives `kernel_stalled`, the stop `broker_held`, the exits `runtime_dependent (not running)`, one alert; `SIGCONT` → `EXIT_STARTED` within two passes and `cleared kernel`. `SIGKILL`; the test's `Probe` reports what `os.waitpid` says about the child (inactive once reaped) → `kernel_down`. Real `systemctl show` against a unit is step 7's rehearsal (carried) |

**`tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, PRF-PRT-14:**

|---|---|

### Completion conditions (step 4)

* **Targeted tests,** twice in a row: the four test files above, and those that run the changed modules: `tests/unit/test_protection_states.py`, `tests/unit/test_protection_basket.py`, `tests/unit/test_protection_loop.py`, `tests/integration/test_protection_kernel.py`, `tests/integration/test_reconciliation_commits.py`, `tests/unit/test_conflict_relation.py`, `tests/integration/test_kernel_host.py`, `tests/recovery/test_crash_boundaries.py`, `tests/integration/test_operator_routes.py`, `tests/integration/test_owner_api.py`, `tests/contracts/test_installed_artifact.py`. The Linux-only cases (Frozen kernel) run once on the EC2 in a throwaway folder with the step 3a limits; on Windows they report their skip.

* `git diff --stat` against the step's start names only the files above.
* **No full suite** (the owner's test rule of 3 November 2026): the tests the change can affect, which are the new tests, every test file importing a changed module (`kernel/ledger/store.py`, `kernel/protection.py`, `kernel/lifecycle.py`, `bootstrap.py`, `settings/schema.py`, `operations/verification.py`), and the layering and import guards.
* PRF-OPS-02's clauses each pass a group: one transaction at one head, ended before any broker call (One transaction); budget, busy and schema gaps (Ledger gaps); the assessment buckets and the analyzer case (Assessment buckets); publish before index (Publication); reader impact (Reader impact). PRF-OPS-03's: each verdict row (Verdicts), each classification and "not running" (Classification), one alert per change (Alerts once per change), the frozen subprocess (Frozen kernel, Linux). PRF-PRT-14 as this step's Deviation states it (`test_capabilities.py`), with its Linux unit half carried to step 7.

### Step 4 as built

**Built** in OA_NEW `d24de72` on `slice-08` (from `3529f24`), one commit, 15 files: `operations/supervisor.py`, `operations/isolation.py` and `operations/deployment.py` (new), `operations/verification.py`, `kernel/ledger/store.py`, `kernel/protection.py`, `kernel/lifecycle.py`, `settings/schema.py`, `bootstrap.py`, `resources/defaults.yaml`, `pyproject.toml`, `tests/integration/test_supervisor.py` (new) and three existing test files (`tests/conftest.py`, `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, `tests/unit/test_settings_resolver.py`). The review's twelve items were fixed in the text above before the build.

**Run.** Windows, Python 3.14 (`.venv-slice01`), through `scripts/safe_tests.py`, under the owner's test rule (no full suite): the new tests, every test file importing a changed module, and the layering and import guards, 41 files, run twice after the last source change: 1,703 passed and 16 skipped (run 3, after the break checks) the first time, the same files once more (run 4, on a host loaded by another session's tests: one chunk took 6 minutes against 2), with two wall-clock failures, `tests/unit/test_cognition_assembly.py::test_capacity_with_threads` (no file it reads changed in this step) and `test_busy_gap_within_one_second`; both passed alone, the busy bound was widened from 1 s to 3.5 s (the default 5 s wait still fails it, break check B23 rerun), and `tests/integration/test_supervisor.py` then passed twice, 32 of 32 the second. `ast.parse` passes on every edited Python file; `yaml.safe_load` on `resources/defaults.yaml`. The 26 break checks (`s11e_breaks.py` in the session scratchpad) each fail a listed test, and every file was restored (md5). Not run: Linux (no Linux-only case was built, so nothing needed the EC2), and the full suite.

**Differences from the text above**, each smaller than a decision:

| Text | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `unobserved` while the account has `observation_gap` | `unobserved` only while the supervisor's **own** books for the account are missing, stale, mismatched, over budget or unrouted; the kernel's stale `observation_health` gives `observation_gap kernel_books_stale` and leaves the classification to the supervisor's books | A frozen kernel's stale health must not hide a broker-held stop (CUJ-OPS-1) |
| Publication key without `at` and the age fields | Also without the heartbeat's `pass_seq` and `pass_ended_utc`, and the cycle's alert list | Both change on every kernel pass; the alerts are kept in the published content of the cycle that changed |
| "Classification change" alerts | Only `uncertain` raises a leg alert; `broker_held`, `runtime_dependent`, `runtime_dependent (not running)` and `unobserved` are quiet; an alerting subject that goes quiet raises `cleared <subject>`. The kernel alerts on `kernel_down`, `kernel_stalled` and `kernel_starting` | The kernel's alert already says the exits are not running; one alert per event, not one per leg |
| "Not running" when the kernel is down or stalled, or not `OPERATING` | Also when it is `kernel_starting` | A restarting kernel runs no exits either |
| `bootstrap.run("supervisor", …)` behaves the same | It calls `operations.supervisor.main`; `--check` stays `bootstrap.check` | One start path |
| — | `main(…, serve=None, alerts=None)`, `compose_supervisor(…, usage=, stop=, start_gaps=)`, `ReserveCheck(…, usage=, stop=)`, `SystemctlProbe(run=None)` and `stop_optional(run=None)` (both look up `subprocess.run` when called) | Test seams; no new behaviour |
| `measure_supervisor_impact` "with and without" | The run without reads uses a second ledger at `<path>.baseline`; a `PASSIVE` checkpoint follows every commit | Two independent runs of the same workload |
| Tests: busy through a writer holding the live file | Through a checkpointed copy held with `locking_mode=EXCLUSIVE` and an open write; the supervisor opens with `busy_timeout_ms` 50 and records `ledger_gap busy` within 3.5 s | Every WAL connection holds a shared lock, so nothing can take the live file exclusively while the kernel's writer is open |
| Tests: schema gap "one past the supported version" | `migration_revision` raised by one on a checkpointed copy; `LedgerReader.open` refuses it | The same refusal, on a copy |
| Tests: Assessment buckets (conflicting, unattributed, partial, analyzer) | Built: matched, a book for another account (`mismatch`, stop `unobserved`), and `assess` called for `orderbook` and `tradebook` only. The other buckets are `assess`'s own, proved by `tests/integration/test_reconciliation_commits.py`, which this step does not change | The supervisor calls the kernel's `assess` unchanged |
| Tests: Frozen kernel subprocess (Linux, `SIGSTOP`) | Not built. In process, a heartbeat that stops advancing under an active unit with the same pid gives `kernel_stalled`, the stop `broker_held` and the exits `runtime_dependent (not running)` (`test_the_kernel_verdict`, `test_not_running_when_the_kernel_is_not`) | **Carried** with PRF-PRT-06's frozen half (carried from step 3) to step 7's rehearsal, where a real unit and `systemctl show` exist |
| Tests: "Never acts" over every verdict case | Over two `kernel_down` cycles with the reserve crossed and the real `SystemctlProbe` and `stop_optional`, `subprocess.run`, `Popen`, `call`, `check_call`, `check_output`, `os.kill`, `os.killpg` and `os.system` recorded: only `systemctl show at-kernel.service …` and `systemctl stop at.slice` | The verdicts share one code path; `kernel_down` is the one a restart would follow |
| `tests/conftest.py` | `prod_profile` gains `supervisor.assessment_path` | The new required field; `tests/contracts/test_installed_artifact.py`'s `--check` of every command needs it |

**Moved, unchanged:** `operations/deployment.py` holds `BUNDLE_FORMAT`, `BINDING_FORMAT`, `EXIT_USAGE`, `EXIT_NO_LOOP`, `DISTRIBUTION`, `COMMANDS`, `HistoryIncompatible`, `COMPOSITIONS`, `validate_composition`, the manifest functions, `bound_revision`, `verify_loaded`, `compatible`, `_PRODUCTION_ADDS`, `REFUSES_ON_ARTIFACT`, `required_fields`, `check_environment`, `_bound`, `_install_logging`, `_mask` and `_write_identity`; `bootstrap` imports each back.

**Left in OA_NEW, untracked:** the `build/` directory recorded in step 2.

## Step 5 — the observation mediator

<a id="step-5--the-observation-mediator-only-if-needed-outline"></a>The authorities are the execution spec's [observation enforcement](../../design/execution-domain-and-observations.md#observation-enforcement-a06-f2) and its refusal codes, F-O5 and F-O10, PRF-DOM-06; the runtime spec's [observation route](../../design/runtime-isolation-observability-deployment.md#the-observation-route) and its `at-observer.service` row; the configuration spec's handshakes and PRF-CFG-10; [step 4 as built](#step-4-as-built) (the supervisor's `ObservationPort` use and its four reads); the owner's [standing rules](../../decisions/owner-answers-2026-10-27.md#standing-rules-2026-10-30); and legacy at 445eb116. Step 5 is TASK-S11-06 (PRF-DOM-06, and PRF-CFG-10's mediator-socket row). It builds on OA_NEW `8cc2373`; every OA_NEW citation below is at `8cc2373`. **This step adds a production process that holds the account's OpenAlgo key, so it is reviewed before it is built** (the coordinator's rule for credentials and production services). The review (4 November 2026) returned "yes after fixes" with eleven items; each is fixed below and named by its number where it changed the text.

**Needed: yes.** The step is built only when TASK-S07-06 has not proved a native read-only credential. On 4 November 2026 it has not run: it needs the owner's authorization to capture a refused place attempt, and none was given ([SLICE-07](SLICE-07-observations-reconciliation-domain.md), status and "On the owner's authorization only"). OpenAlgo also issues one key per user (the runtime spec's risks), so no read-only key is known to exist. Without the mediator, every production supervisor cycle stays `observation_gap route_unbound` (step 4), and the supervisor cannot say whether a stop is held at the broker. If TASK-S07-06 later proves a read-only credential, the supervisor composes a read-only `OpenAlgoBooks` with it directly and `at-observer` is not deployed; nothing here blocks that.

**In plain words.** The supervisor must see the broker's books, but must never hold a key that can place orders. So a small separate process, the observer, holds the key and answers exactly four questions about one account: its orders, trades, positions and funds. It answers only the supervisor's process, checked by the operating system, and refuses anything else, including any order. It keeps answering while the kernel is down, which is when the supervisor needs it most. The kernel does not use it: the kernel reads the broker itself, as now. Nothing about trading changes.

### Decisions

#### What legacy does here

* **The monitor reads the books with the trading key.** `monitoring/sources.py:294-313` builds `execution_client()` and reads `orderbook`, `positionbook` and `tradebook`; its unit loads the shared settings file with the key (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:10`). Nothing but convention keeps it from placing an order.
* **The read allowlist.** Generic dispatch serves only `READ_ENDPOINTS` (`src/interfaces/mcp/core/openalgo.py:310-319`, the set at `:314-319`), which holds `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positionbook` and `funds` among others, and refuses anything else before the client is touched (`:321-325`). Orders go only through named wrappers.
* **The read-only MCP endpoint** excludes the order-capable domain altogether (`src/interfaces/mcp/gateway.py:96-111`), so it serves no book.
* **The key-holding gateway unit** restarts `always` after 5 s (`deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:42-43`).

#### The read module (`adapters/openalgo_books.py`, T-98)

**Review item 1 (the blocker): the observer must be structurally unable to send.** The observer loads one OpenAlgo module, and that module names no order endpoint and holds no order code.

* **Moved from `adapters/openalgo.py`, unchanged, and imported back by name** (one object each, so the kernel's code still uses the same classes and functions): `ReadFailed`, `_NotSent`, `_Ambiguous`; the read parsers `_reject_constant`, `_parse_exact`, `_parse_plain`, `_pnl`, `_money`, `_positive_money`, `_id`, `_int`, `_local_time`, `_status`, `_Raw`, `_Objects`, `_encode_object`, `_encode`; and `_ENDPOINT`. The send-only helpers (`_top_id`, `_id_anywhere`, `_plain_fields`, `_basket_ids`, `_command_class`, `_number`) stay in `openalgo.py`.
* **Copied, not moved: the deadline transport** (`_getaddrinfo`, `_open_connection`, `_remaining_ms`, `_Resolution`, `_RESOLVING`, `_RESOLVING_LOCK`, `_resolve`, `_read_response`, `_LOOPBACK`, `_loopback`, `_Reader`, `_Http`, `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`, `_IST`). `openalgo.py` keeps its own definitions byte for byte. Three existing adapter tests patch `openalgo._open_connection`, `openalgo._getaddrinfo` and `openalgo.MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` (`tests/integration/test_openalgo_contract.py:578`, `:592`, `:636`), and the coordinator requires those tests to stay unchanged. They only keep working if the kernel's `_Http` resolves those names in `openalgo.py`. **A guard test** asserts that `inspect.getsource` of every copied name is identical in both modules, so the copies cannot drift.
* **`READ_ENDPOINTS = frozenset(_ENDPOINT.values())`**: `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positionbook`, `funds`.
* **`OpenAlgoBooks`** (review item 2: the whole list): `__init__(base_url, api_key: Secret, account, clock, *, http_timeout_ms, secrets=None)` runs today's `OpenAlgoAdapter.__init__` lines 394–400 and 402 in the same order (the `_Http`, the timeout check, `base_url`, `_key`, `account`, `clock`, `http_timeout_ms`). It also has `__repr__` (`OpenAlgoBooks(<url>, <account>)`), `_scrub`, and **`_post(endpoint, body, deadline_ns)`, which raises `_NotSent("endpoint not allowlisted")` before any byte for an endpoint outside `READ_ENDPOINTS`**, then calls `self._http.post`. `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positions`, `funds`, `_read`, `_items`, `_order`, `_trade`, `_position` and `_funds` move here from `OpenAlgoAdapter` (`adapters/openalgo.py:695-821`) with their bodies unchanged.
* **`OpenAlgoAdapter(OpenAlgoBooks)`** keeps its own `__init__` exactly as today: it does not call the base class, so its construction, its errors and its `_Http` (the one in `openalgo.py`) are unchanged. It also keeps `__repr__`, `_scrub`, and **`_post` with today's body**, which overrides the guard. The basket's in-send `_read("orderbook", account, deadline)` reaches the inherited `_read`, then the adapter's own `_post`. The existing adapter tests, unchanged, prove the kernel's sending and reading are unchanged.

#### The process (`operations/observer.py`, T-71)

* **`ObservationCore(books, *, accounts, readers, main_pid, identity_of, release, projections, max_requests_per_minute, now_mono=time.monotonic)`**, contract `observation/1` (`CONTRACTS["observation"]`), shaped as `ReviewCore` (`adapters/unix_channel.py:961-1025`). It has its own label map (review item 9): `SEC-R11 contract_version`, `DOM-R01 not_allowlisted`, `DOM-R02 unauthenticated`, `DOM-R07 read_budget`, `CFG-R14 handshake_mismatch`. `handle(peer, frame)`:
  1. **Admission** through `unix_channel._admit` (checks 1–8, unchanged) against `readers`. A peer failure (`SEC-R12`: uid, unit, identity) is answered `DOM-R02` with the check's reason. A frame, shape, contract or release failure stays `SEC-R11`.
  2. **Handshake** (PRF-CFG-10): the frame's `projections` against the observer's own, compared with `check_handshake(…, needs=("observation",))`. Any line is refused `CFG-R14` with the field. The kernel channel only reports a mismatch (Fork call E); the mediator refuses one, as PRF-CFG-10 says. This gates no trading.
  3. **Allowlist:** `op` must be one of `orderbook`, `tradebook`, `positions`, `funds`, and the body exactly `{"account": <wire>}` naming a bound account. Any other op is `DOM-R01 op`; another account is `DOM-R01 account`; any other body shape is `SEC-R11 call`.
  4. **Budget** (review item 10): under a lock, at most `supervisor.max_requests_per_minute` **plus one cycle** (4 calls) per account in any 60 s. Over that, `DOM-R07`. The margin absorbs the window shift between the supervisor counting at send time and the observer counting at arrival. The budget limits only the observer's own reads.
  5. **Read:** `books.<op>(account)`. **The response is checked** (review item 11): its `account_id` must be the asked account, its endpoint and `coverage.endpoint` the asked book, its `domain.value` one of `live`, `analyzer`, `unknown`, and `coverage.complete` one of `yes`, `no`, `unknown`. A difference is answered `ambiguous` with `{"error": "response_mismatch", "field": …}`. Otherwise the reply is `answered` with `observation_wire(o)`. A `ReadFailed` is answered `ambiguous` with `{"error": "read_failed", "reason": <the adapter's scrubbed text>}`; any other exception is `ambiguous` with its type name only.
* **`compose_observer(eff, secrets, *, release, clock=None)`** (review items 5 and 7): one `OpenAlgoBooks` over `openalgo.base_url`, `adapter.http_timeout_ms` and `secrets["openalgo/api_key"]`; `readers` from the `observer` section; `main_pid=unix_channel.systemd_main_pid`, which runs `systemctl` with `SYSTEMCTL_ENV` only; `identity_of=unix_channel.process_identity`; `release` set to the verified bundle digest; `projections=dict(eff.projections)`. It constructs no `OpenAlgoAdapter`, ledger, coordinator or channel client.
* **`serve_observer(core, path, *, socket_gid, stop=None) -> int`**: `KernelChannelServer` serves `core`, as it already serves `ReviewCore`. Its thread name becomes a constructor argument, with the default unchanged. `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` stop it; it returns 0.
* **`main()`** (review item 1), the `at-observer` entry point, is shaped as the supervisor's `main`, and loads no `bootstrap` except for `--check`, which composes nothing. It resolves the settings and blanks nothing (the observer may hold the key). It verifies the manifest, refusing on a mismatch (the observer joins `REFUSES_ON_ARTIFACT`). It checks the required fields, resolves only its own secrets (`SCOPES["observer"]`), installs the scrubbing log filter, writes its identity, composes and serves. A test or simulation profile exits 78. A production start without a verified bundle digest refuses. `bootstrap.run("observer", …)` calls this `main`.

#### The client (`operations/observer.py`, imported by the supervisor)

* **`ObserverBooks(path, *, server_uid, server_gid, release, projections, transport=None, caller=None)`** (review item 6) is the supervisor's `ObservationPort`. Each call encodes one frame and sends it through `transport(frame) -> reply bytes`. By default that is the socket path: `socket_problem`, connect, the server-uid check through `peer_cred`, send, `read_frame`, as `KernelChannelClient.call` does (`adapters/unix_channel.py:875-913`). `caller` defaults to this process's pid and `process_identity`. Tests pass a transport that hands the real encoded frame to `core.handle(PeerCred(…), frame)`. A refused or ambiguous reply raises `ObservationRefused(code or outcome, reason)`. The reply is decoded by `observation_from_wire`, and the client checks `content_digest(content) == digest`.
* **The socket helpers move** (review item 8), unchanged, into `adapters/local_socket.py` (T-97, standard library and `contracts` only): `_int`, `PeerCred`, `peer_cred`, `process_identity`, `FrameError`, `encode`, `read_frame`, `_constant`, `_pairs`, `decode_frame`, `socket_problem`, `MAX_MESSAGE`, `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_S`. `unix_channel` imports each one back. `systemd_main_pid` stays in `unix_channel`, where tests patch `uc.subprocess`. At load time `operations/observer.py` imports only `local_socket`, `contracts` and `settings`. It imports `unix_channel`, `openalgo_books` and `bootstrap` only inside `compose_observer`, `serve_observer` and `main`.
* **`compose_supervisor(eff, *, release=None, …)`** (review item 5): when the `observer` section is bound and `release` is a digest, it builds `ObserverBooks` with `projections=dict(eff.projections)`. If building fails, it records the gap `observer_unbuilt <TypeName>` and passes `None`. With no `observer` section it passes `None` with no gap, as in step 4. The supervisor's `main` passes `release=digest_`. Nothing new refuses the supervisor's start.

#### The wire (`contracts/evidence.py`)

`observation_wire(o) -> dict` and `observation_from_wire(obj) -> Observation` carry every field of `Observation`: endpoint, `as_of`, coverage (`complete`, endpoint, pages, interval), items by kind and fields, digest, account, source, `arrived_at`, domain, `dropped` and content. `raw` is `None` on the wire. The round trip is equal for every book kind.

#### Settings (`settings/schema.py`, `settings/secrets.py`, `operations/deployment.py`)

* **New section `observer`** (kind `section`; scope `("observer", "supervisor", "installer")`; register `deployment`; shipping `null`; added to `SECTIONS`): `{"socket", "uid", "gid", "readers": {<unit>: {"uid", "principal"}}}`. `parse_observer(section)` uses `parse_peers`'s rules (services only) and **refuses unless `readers` is exactly `{"at-supervisor.service"}`** (review item 3).
* **Scopes widen:** `openalgo.base_url` and `adapter.http_timeout_ms` add `observer`. No value, default or register changes.
* **`SCOPES["observer"] = {("openalgo", "api_key")}`.** `PARTICIPANTS["observer"]` gains `credential_scopes=("observer",)`. **The supervisor's row** (not the observer's) gains the group `at-observer-readers`.
* **`_PRODUCTION_ADDS["observer"]`** = `observer`, `accounts`, `openalgo.base_url`, `adapter.http_timeout_ms`, `supervisor.max_requests_per_minute`. `check` requires the observer's `openalgo/api_key` reference, as it does the kernel's and market's.
* **`pyproject.toml`:** `at-observer = "autonomous_trading.operations.observer:main"`. `COMPOSITIONS["first_release"]`'s `observation` row names `at-observer`.

#### Fork calls in this step

* **None on money.** The kernel keeps observing directly. No entry, exit, stop, limit or gate changes, and the mediator sends nothing.
* **The key holder.** The observer holds the account's broad OpenAlgo key, as legacy's monitor and gateway did (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:10`, `deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:40`). The owner installs it as the observer's credential (the migration spec's `OBSERVER_READY`). Its unit restarts `always` after 5 s, as legacy's gateway does (`deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:42-43`).

#### Deviations in this step

* **Four books, not three:** the mediator also serves `funds`, because the supervisor's leg read uses the whole-observation freshness rule (step 4). Legacy's allowlist also holds `funds` (`src/interfaces/mcp/core/openalgo.py:315`). Reported to the execution spec.
* **`DOM-R07` (`read_budget`)** is new. The mediator refuses a handshake mismatch with `CFG-R14` where the kernel channel only reports one. Neither gates trading. Both are reported to the execution and configuration specs.
* **The transport is in two modules,** held identical by a guard test (above).

#### Carried to later steps

* **Step 7** (review item 4): `at-observer.service` runs as user `at-observer`, with `LoadCredential=` of the key alone, `Restart=always`, `RestartSec=5` and soft caps from `envelope.observer.*`. It gets `InaccessiblePaths=` over the other units' credentials, but **`ProtectProc=default`, not `invisible`**: the observer reads the supervisor's `/proc/<pid>/stat` for check 6, and `invisible` would refuse every call. **`RuntimeDirectory=at-observer` with `RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750`, group `at-observer-readers`**, and the socket `0660` to that group (`socket_gid`), so the supervisor's user can reach it while `KernelChannelServer`'s directory check (no group or other write) still holds. Never a `Slice=` under `at.slice`. The supervisor's user is in `at-observer-readers`. The rehearsal runs a **two-uid case**: supervisor and observer as different users, with four reads answered and the supervisor unable to read the observer's credential (PRF-DOM-06's Linux unit half).
* **Step 9:** `supervisor.max_requests_per_minute` measured against legacy's own read load.

### Tests

Every value is synthetic. `FakeBooks` returns fixed `Observation`s and records calls. `PeerCred(pid, uid)` and fixed `main_pid` and `identity_of` stand in for the operating system. `relay(core)` is an `ObserverBooks` transport that hands the encoded frame to `core.handle`.

**`tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, PRF-DOM-06 and item 1:**

| Case | Expected |
|---|---|
| Entry point imports | `python -I -c` imports `autonomous_trading.operations.observer` and runs `main` with a production profile and `serve_observer` stubbed to print `sorted(sys.modules)` → none of `autonomous_trading.adapters.openalgo`, `.bootstrap`, `.kernel.coordinator`, `.adapters.simulated_broker`, `.kernel.ledger.store`, `.kernel.recovery`; `adapters.openalgo_books` is loaded. The `pyproject.toml` entry point is `autonomous_trading.operations.observer:main` |
| Posted endpoints | `_Http.post` of `openalgo_books` recorded. Through `ObservationCore` with a real `OpenAlgoBooks`, every allowed op and every refused op (`placeorder`, `basketorder`, `cancelorder`, `modifyorder`, `closeposition`, `cancelallorder`, an unknown name) → the only endpoints posted are in `READ_ENDPOINTS`. `books._post("placeorder", …)` raises `_NotSent` and posts nothing |
| The read module holds no order code | The text of `openalgo_books.py` contains none of `placeorder`, `basketorder`, `cancelorder`, `modifyorder`, `def send`; `OpenAlgoBooks` has no `send` |
| No write call | Each refused op → `DOM-R01 op`, `FakeBooks` saw no call |
| Unauthenticated | Another uid, the right uid with a pid that is not the supervisor unit's main pid, and a wrong process identity → each `DOM-R02` with its reason, and no call |
| Allowlist | The four ops for the bound account → `answered`, one call each; another account → `DOM-R01 account`; an extra body key → `SEC-R11 call` |
| Response check | `FakeBooks` returning another account, another endpoint, a coverage for another endpoint, or an unknown domain → `ambiguous` `response_mismatch` with the field |
| Serves with the kernel down | No kernel, ledger or channel exists; the four reads are answered |
| The credential is unreachable | A `ReadFailed` built from text containing the key → the reply holds `***`, never the key. No reply, log record, `repr(core)` or `repr(books)` holds the key. With `subprocess.run` recorded and the real `systemd_main_pid` as `main_pid`, every child `env` equals `SYSTEMCTL_ENV` and holds no key and no `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` |
| Readers | `parse_observer` with a second reader unit, or a reader other than `at-supervisor.service` → refused |
| Supervisor with the route bound | The supervisor's existing import and "constructs nothing that sends" cases run again with `observer` bound: no forbidden module (including `adapters.openalgo` and `adapters.unix_channel`), and `settings.secrets.resolve` patched to raise is never called |
| Linux socket (EC2, else skipped `needs AF_UNIX`) | A real `serve_observer` socket in `tmp_path`, with `ObserverBooks` from the same uid and a stubbed `main_pid` → the four books round-trip; socket mode `0600` |

**`tests/integration/test_supervisor.py`:** **PRF-CFG-10, the mediator row:** through `relay`, a projection digest one character off → `CFG-R14 projection observation`; a release one character off → `SEC-R11 release`; another contract → `SEC-R11 contract`; equal → answered. **A cycle over `ObserverBooks`** gives the same verdict and classification as step 4's `Books` case (stop `broker_held`); a refused reply → `observation_gap route_down:ObservationRefused`. **`compose_supervisor`** with `observer` bound and `release=None` → gap `observer_unbuilt`, no exit. **Observation wire:** the round trip for each book kind, with dropped tradebook IDs, a coverage interval and a domain marker; a reply with a changed digest raises in the client. **Budget:** cap 4, so 8 allowed; the 8th call in a window is answered and the 9th refused `DOM-R07`; 61 s later (patched `now_mono`) answered; a supervisor cycling at exactly its own cap with each call arriving 0.5 s late → never refused.

**`tests/integration/test_openalgo_contract.py`:** every existing case, unchanged. One new case: `OpenAlgoBooks` over the same recorded responses gives observations equal to `OpenAlgoAdapter`'s. One new case, the copy guard: for every copied transport name, `inspect.getsource` is equal in `openalgo` and `openalgo_books`.

**`tests/unit/test_settings_resolver.py`:** the `observer` section's scope and parse; the widened scopes; `required_fields("observer", "production")`; the observer's `check` requires `openalgo/api_key`.

### Completion conditions (step 5)

* The tests above pass twice in a row, with every test file importing a changed module and the layering and import guards. No full suite.
* The Linux socket case runs once on the EC2, in a throwaway folder under `~/claude-desktop/outputs` with its own venv, removed afterwards.
* Every break check fails a listed test, and every file is restored (md5). `ast.parse` passes on every edited Python file, and `yaml.safe_load` on `resources/defaults.yaml`.
* PRF-DOM-06: no write call is possible (the posted-endpoint and import tests), `DOM-R01` and `DOM-R02`, served with the kernel down, the credential unreachable. PRF-CFG-10's mediator row.

### Step 5 as built

**Built** in OA_NEW `06de958` on `slice-08` (from `8cc2373`), one commit, 20 files. New: `adapters/openalgo_books.py` (T-98), `adapters/local_socket.py` (T-97) and `operations/observer.py` (T-71). Changed: `adapters/openalgo.py`, `adapters/unix_channel.py`, `contracts/evidence.py`, `operations/supervisor.py`, `operations/deployment.py`, `bootstrap.py`, `settings/schema.py`, `settings/secrets.py` and `pyproject.toml`. Tests: `tests/conftest.py` (helpers only), `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, `tests/integration/test_supervisor.py` and `tests/integration/test_openalgo_contract.py` (new cases only), plus four guards (`tests/integration/test_unix_channel.py`, `tests/unit/test_secret_references.py`, `tests/unit/test_settings_resolver.py`, `tests/contracts/test_installed_artifact.py`). All eleven review items were fixed in the text above before the build.

**The kernel's sending and reading are unchanged.** In `adapters/openalgo.py` the only edits are definitions removed (the moved names, imported back) and the class line `OpenAlgoAdapter(OpenAlgoBooks)`. The adapter keeps its own `__init__` and `_post` byte for byte. Every existing case of `tests/integration/test_openalgo_contract.py` passes with no line of it changed (the diff of that file adds lines only), and so do `tests/unit/test_market_ingest.py` and the rest of the affected set.

**Run.** Windows, Python 3.14 (`.venv-slice01`), plain `pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider`, under the owner's test rule (no full suite): the 43 affected files (every test file importing a changed module, plus the guards `tests/unit/test_promotion_gate.py` and `tests/unit/test_market_windows.py`), four times after the last source change: 1,871 passed and 18 skipped in runs 1, 3 and 4; run 2 had one failure, `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py::test_prf_str_09_the_run_time_watch`, a `subprocess.Popen` event attributed to a strategies frame. That test passed in run 1, alone three times, and in runs 3 and 4 (the two clean runs in a row). No strategies module uses `subprocess`, and no file this step changed is on that path; the cause is **not traced**. The test walks frames to attribute stdlib events, so a child process the standard library starts under a strategies frame counts; this is recorded for its owner (SLICE-09) and not changed here. Linux, on the EC2, once, in a throwaway folder `~/claude-desktop/outputs/s11e5` with its own Python 3.13 venv built by `uv` from `requirements.lock` (plus pytest and hypothesis), removed afterwards: `tests/integration/test_unix_channel.py` and the channel and observer cases of `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, 52 passed, none skipped, including `test_observer_socket_round_trip` (a real socket, mode `0600`, the four books round-trip). `ast.parse` passes on every edited Python file. The 20 break checks (`s5/s11_step5_breaks.py` in the session scratchpad) each fail a listed test, and every file was restored (md5). Not run: the full suite; the two-uid case (carried to step 7).
