#### Users, groups and directories

**System users** (`sysusers.d/autonomous-trading.conf`): one per unit-bearing participant of the composition (`at-kernel`, `at-market`, `at-supervisor`, `at-observer`, `at-strategy`, `at-operator`, `at-backup`; cognition, MCP, console and research only when composed), and **`at-installer`** (new): the installer participant's user, home `release.root`, no login shell. The owner runs every installer command as it (`sudo -u at-installer …`, with the owner's own sudo; no rule grants that hop). A real run of any command refuses (exit 2) any other user, as legacy refuses all but the checkout's owner (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:2038-2047`); the two root commands are below.

**Groups (seven):** `at-channel`, `at-operators`, `at-ledger-readers`, `at-market-readers`, `at-observer-readers`, and new `at-identity` and `at-assessment-readers`. `PARTICIPANTS` (`settings/schema.py`) changes:

| Participant | Groups after this step | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `kernel` | `at-channel`, `at-market-readers`, `at-identity`, `at-operators` | It sets its channel socket's group to `at-channel` (OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:1051-1052`, `adapters/unix_channel.py:672`), which needs membership; `at-operators` only to look for the kill file in `<paths.run>/kill` (`2770`) |
| `market`, `strategy`, `cognition`, `mcp`, `console` | their own, plus `at-identity` | Each writes `<paths.run>/identity/<participant>.json`; the market also writes its heartbeat `<paths.run>/health/market.json` (OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:1394`) |
| `operator` | `at-channel`, `at-ledger-readers`, `at-identity` (**not** `at-operators`) | Its CLI socket is created in `<paths.run>/operator`, setgid `at-operators`, so the socket already has that group and the `chown` to it (OA_NEW `interfaces/api.py:906`) changes nothing, which Linux allows without membership |
| `supervisor` | `at-ledger-readers`, `at-market-readers`, `at-observer-readers`, `at-identity` | As step 5, plus identity |
| `observer` | `at-identity`; **primary group `at-observer-readers`** (`Group=` in its unit) | `RuntimeDirectory=` takes the unit's `Group=`, so the socket directory's group is `at-observer-readers` (the review's item 3) |
| `backup` | `at-ledger-readers`, `at-market-readers`, `at-assessment-readers` | Its scope already includes `supervisor.assessment_path` |
| `installer` | user `at-installer`; `at-ledger-readers`, `at-market-readers`, `at-assessment-readers`, `at-identity` | The boundary's ledger and calendar reads, the broker read through the assessment, convergence through the identity records and the heartbeat |

**The directory table**, rendered by `render` and written by `apply-root`. `apply-root` creates a missing directory with its owner, group and mode; it **refuses** (`CFG-R10 directory <path>`) an existing one that differs or is a symlink, and changes nothing that exists, so a wrong existing directory is the owner's to fix. `DEPLOYMENT_ROOT` is `/srv/autonomous-trading`, fixed in `deploy/install.py` and rendered as `AT_DEPLOYMENT_ROOT` (step 1's "fixed deployment root"; legacy's kill file resolves against it, OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:1221-1226`).

| Path | Owner | Group | Mode | Who writes; who reads |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DEPLOYMENT_ROOT` | root | root | `0755` | Traversal only |
| `release.root` | `at-installer` | `at-installer` | `0755` | The installer writes; every unit reads its release (group and other write off throughout) |
| `/usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/copies` | root | root | `0755` | The root copies (below) |
| `/var/lib/autonomous-trading` | root | root | `0755` | `apply-root`'s own record `applied.jsonl` (`0644`) and its saved files `installed/<release_id>/` (`0700`) |
| `/etc/autonomous-trading/credentials` and one `<participant>/` per key holder | root | root | `0700` | The owner installs each key file, root `0600` (step 1's "key file `0600`": systemd reads it for `LoadCredential=` and hands the unit a copy owned by the unit's user under `/run/credentials/<unit>/`). The installer never writes a credential |
| `paths.run` | root | root | `0755` | Traversal only; nothing creates entries at this level |
| `<paths.run>/kill`, the kill-file directory (`risk_guard.kill_switch_file` binds `<paths.run>/kill/KILL`; legacy's default was `<run>/KILL`) | root | `at-operators` | `2770` (setgid, **not** sticky) | The `at` command (humans in `at-operators`) creates and removes kill files; any member can remove any member's file, as any legacy process could remove `run/KILL` (every legacy unit ran as the one app user, and `at kill-switch on` wrote `run/KILL` as that user, `deploy/at:4239-4262`, `deploy/systemd/*` `User=__APP_USER__`). The kernel only checks for the file, which needs search permission on the directory: the kernel joins `at-operators` for that (the third review's item 3) |
| `<paths.run>/kernel` | `at-kernel` | `at-channel` | `2750` (setgid) | The channel socket `0660` and its lock (code); `KernelChannelServer`'s directory check holds (owner its uid, no group or other write, OA_NEW `adapters/unix_channel.py:640-643`) |
| `<paths.run>/health` | root | `at-identity` | `3770` (setgid, sticky) | The kernel's and market's heartbeats, `0640` by code (OA_NEW `kernel/host.py:211-215`; `bootstrap.py:1394`); sticky, so neither replaces the other's; the supervisor and installer read |
| `<paths.run>/operator` (the operator's `cli.socket` directory) | `at-operator` | `at-operators` | `2750` | The `at` command's socket, `0660` group `at-operators` by code (OA_NEW `interfaces/api.py:878`, `:904-907`); humans in `at-operators` connect |
| `<paths.run>/cognition` (when cognition is composed) | `at-cognition` | `at-channel` | `2750` | The review socket (OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:1464`), `0660` group `at-channel` by code; the strategy host connects |
| `<paths.run>/identity` | root | `at-identity` | `3770` (setgid, sticky) | Each participant's record, `0640` by code (OA_NEW `operations/deployment.py:401-412`); sticky, so only a record's writer can replace it, and convergence also checks each record's owner is its participant's user |
| `<paths.run>/supervisor` | `at-supervisor` | `at-supervisor` | `0700` | The alert state |
| Parent of `ledger.path` | `at-kernel` | `at-ledger-readers` | `2750` | The ledger and its `-wal` and `-shm`, `0640` under the kernel's `UMask=0027` (Fork call 9; SQLite gives the `-wal` and `-shm` the database file's mode) |
| `paths.evidence` | `at-kernel` | `at-ledger-readers` | `2750` | Kernel evidence |
| `<paths.evidence>/strategies` | `at-strategy` | `at-ledger-readers` | `2750` | Each strategy host's store `<id>/host.sqlite` (OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:1430`), under `UMask=0077` |
| Parent of `market.store_path` | `at-market` | `at-market-readers` | `2750` | The market store, `0640` under `UMask=0027` |
| `supervisor.assessment_path` (its own filesystem, `check` below) | `at-supervisor` | `at-assessment-readers` | `2750` | Assessments and the index, `0640` under `UMask=0027`; the installer and backup read |
| `paths.scratch` | root | root | `0755` | — |
| `<paths.scratch>/<participant>`, one per unit | the participant's user | the same | `0700` | That unit's scratch |
| `/run/at-observer` | `at-observer` (systemd's `RuntimeDirectory=`) | `at-observer-readers` | `0750` | The observer's socket `0660` (code) |
| The backup's staging directory | as the migration spec names it (step 8) | | | Appended by step 8; until then the backup unit gets only the rows above and is not enabled (its destination is unbound) |

**Path rules, an allowlist** (`render`, `check`, `apply-root` and `isolated_work.py` under sudo refuse, `CFG-R10 path <field>`): every path absolute and resolved, and under `/srv/autonomous-trading`, `/var/lib/autonomous-trading` or `/run`; **no component** ending in `.d`, and none named or containing `systemd`, `sudoers` or `polkit`; none inside `release.root`, the copies or the credentials directory; no row inside another row except as listed. So no setting can make `apply-root` create a directory systemd, sudo or polkit reads (the re-review's `assessment_path=/etc/systemd/system/at-.service.d`). Every unit runs with `ProtectHome=yes`, which also hides legacy's home and its `.env`. **Under `--rehearse`** the allowlist is `DIR` alone. Any other entry a participant tries to create under `paths.run` fails with `EACCES`; the real-users rows (step 10, owner's word, or a disposable host) start every unit and run one cycle, which is the evidence that the table is complete.

#### Layout and the installer's home

* **The installer travels with the release.** `stage` copies `deploy/` (the installer, the templates) and `scripts/isolated_work.py` (as `<release>/deploy/isolated_work.py`) into the release, and `build_manifest(deploy_dir=…)` records them in `artifact.deploy`: `install_sha256`, each template's digest (step 2, OA_NEW `operations/deployment.py:117-160`), and new `isolated_work_sha256`. Every command run as `at-installer` runs as `<release>/venv/bin/python -I <release>/deploy/install.py`; `check` refuses a release whose `deploy/` files differ from `artifact.deploy` (`CFG-R06`). **Root never runs these files:** root runs only a root copy (below).
* **`release.root`** holds `<release_id>/` (`venv/`, `artifact/` with the wheel and `requirements.lock`, `bundle.json`, `settings.json`, `deploy/`, `units/`), the link `current` and the host log `transitions.jsonl`. `release_id` is `<UTC stamp>-<bundle_digest[:12]>`.
* **The rehearsal** (`--rehearse DIR`): `release.root`, the unit directory, the fake root (`sysusers.d`, polkit, sudoers, the copies, `/var/lib/autonomous-trading`, the directory table) and `systemctl` all point inside `DIR`; `fake_systemctl` ports legacy's (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:1851-1966`), adds `cat` (the unit with its drop-ins, systemd's search order) and keeps per-unit `.state.json` (`ActiveState`, `MainPID`, `UnitFileState`, the release that wrote the unit). A started unit runs its real command ([the rehearsal composition](#the-rehearsal-composition)). A crash hook, `AT_REHEARSAL_CRASH=<state>:<intent|done>`, kills the installer with `SIGKILL` at that record, and is refused (exit 2) outside `--rehearse`.

#### The root copy (`install-root-copy`)

**The build record** (the third review's item 1). The bundle cannot be the trust anchor: `stage` writes it on the host as `at-installer`, and its `artifact` holds host-specific values (the interpreter and SQLite versions, each installed dependency's RECORD hash, OA_NEW `operations/deployment.py:157-167`). So the anchor is **`build-record.json`**, holding only host-independent hashes: `{"format": "at-build-record/1", "git_sha", "wheel_sha256", "lock_sha256", "install_sha256", "isolated_work_sha256", "templates": {<name>: <sha256>}}`. **Who writes it:** `deploy/install.py build-record --wheel W --lock L --deploy D` (standard library only), run by the release build off the host (`.github/workflows/verify.yml`'s build job, which also builds the wheel), which uploads it as a build artifact next to the wheel. **Where it lives:** off the host, in the build's artifacts and in the owner's own copy; the owner notes its sha256 there. On the host it exists only as the root-owned copy the owner places for one run, checked against that noted sha256.

**`install-root-copy --release R --build-record B --profile-digest P`**, run by the owner as root with the owner's own sudo (no rule grants it; it refuses when `SUDO_USER` is `at-installer`). `B` is a root-owned path (beside the bootstrap `install.py`, below) holding the record; `P` is the sha256 of the profile the owner approved.

1. **Inputs checked against the record before anything is built** (`CFG-R06`), each by its own sha256: the wheel (`wheel_sha256`), `requirements.lock` (`lock_sha256`), `deploy/install.py` (`install_sha256`), the release's copy of `scripts/isolated_work.py` (`isolated_work_sha256`) and each template (`templates`, exactly the record's names); the deployment profile against `P`. The full bundle digest is not an input.
2. **Build as root** in `/usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/copies/R/`: a virtual environment from the system interpreter; `pip install --require-hashes --only-binary :all: --no-deps -r requirements.lock` and the wheel (no source distribution, so no setup code runs); `deploy/` copied from the checked files; the profile copied to `profile/production.yaml`.
3. **The copy's own bundle:** the copy's interpreter (now running only verified code) writes `bundle.json` with `build_manifest` and runs `verify_loaded` against it, before `chown`; its digest must equal `R/bundle.json`'s, so the staged release is the one the copy describes. A mismatch removes the copy and refuses.
4. `chown -R root:root`, `chmod -R go-w`, and `root_owned` (step 6) over the copy and every ancestor.
5. Keeps only this copy and the running release's; appends `{release_id, bundle_digest, build_record_sha256, install_sha256, profile_sha256: P, by: SUDO_USER}` to `applied.jsonl`; writes the sudoers files rendered for the copies present ([root-written rules](#root-written-rules)) through `visudo -cqf`. `build_manifest` also records `artifact.lock_sha256` (new), so the bundle digest covers the lock.

**First install** (no copy exists): the owner copies `R/deploy/install.py` to a root-owned path (`/usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/bootstrap/install.py`, root `0644` in a root `0755` directory), checks it with `sha256sum` against the off-host record's `install_sha256`, places the record beside it (root `0644`) after checking the record's own sha256 against the one noted off the host, and only then runs `sudo /usr/bin/python3 -I /usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/bootstrap/install.py install-root-copy …`. **In this mode `install.py` imports nothing from `release.root`:** the `install-root-copy` path uses the standard library only (a guard test runs it with `autonomous_trading` unimportable), reads `R`'s files as data, and runs code from `R` only through the copy's venv after step 1's checks. Later runs come from the running copy. `docs/operations.md` gives the owner's commands.

#### Commands (`deploy/install.py`)

| Command | Runs as | Does | Writes to the host log |
|---|---|---|---|
| `render [--release R]` | `at-installer` | Renders every composition participant's unit, `sysusers.d/autonomous-trading.conf`, the directory table, the two polkit rules and the three sudoers files into `<release>/units/`, plus `units/plan.json`; runs the render checks | No |
| `stage --wheel W --lock L` | `at-installer` | Resource check, then `STAGING` → `STAGED` | Yes |
| `check --release R` | `at-installer` | `VERIFIED_DISARMED` | Yes |
| `activate --release R --authorized-by NAME --instruction TEXT` | `at-installer` | `AUTHORIZED` through `CONVERGED` | Yes |
| `resume` | `at-installer` | Continues the last unfinished transition from its facts | Yes |
| `restore` | `at-installer` | Returns to the previous release | Yes |
| `status` | `at-installer` | The last record, `current`, each unit's release and state, each operation's availability, each value rendered from legacy while unmeasured | No |
| `build-record --wheel W --lock L --deploy D` | the release build, off the host | Writes `build-record.json` | No |
| `install-root-copy --release R --build-record B --profile-digest P` | root (the owner's sudo) | The root copy | `applied.jsonl` |
| `apply-root` | root (sudo from `at-installer`, the copy's exact rule) | The one root step: writes and reloads, never enables | `applied.jsonl`; its facts become `UNITS_WRITTEN`'s |
| `enable-units` | root (sudo from `at-installer`, the copy's second exact rule) | Enables exactly the rendered at-boot units and timers (the backup timer only when its destination is bound), and disables any unit a previous `enable-units` enabled that the rendering no longer has; starts nothing | `applied.jsonl`; its facts become `STARTED`'s |

Exit codes: 0 done; 2 usage or wrong user; 3 refused, with the rule (`CFG-R06`, `R08`, `R09`, `R10`, `R11`, `R12`, `R13`) on standard error and in the record; 4 crashed mid-transition (resume or restore).

#### Rendering (`deploy/install.py render`, `deploy/systemd/*`)

* **Templates** for `at-kernel.service`, `at-market.service`, `at-supervisor.service`, `at-observer.service` (T-72), `at-strategy.service` and `at-strategy.timer` (T-59; SLICE-09 decides whether its `ExecCondition` and preflight stay), `at-operator.service` (SLICE-10's, re-templated to the shared placeholders), `at-backup.service` and `at-backup.timer`, and `at.slice` (step 6, T-79). Placeholders: `__RELEASE__` (`release.root/current`), `__USER__`, `__GROUP__`, `__GROUPS__`, `__ENVIRONMENT__`, `__CREDENTIALS__`, `__INACCESSIBLE__`, `__READWRITE__`. A placeholder left after rendering refuses (legacy's `PLACEHOLDER`, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:289`, `:298-300`).
* **From the participant contract** (step 1): `User=` the participant's `user`; `Group=` the same, **except the observer's `at-observer-readers`**; `SupplementaryGroups=` its `groups`; `Environment=` only `AT_PROFILE`, `AT_RELEASE`, `AT_DEPLOYMENT_ROOT` where the contract lists it, the participant's `launcher_env` and the environment names of its in-scope path fields; `LoadCredential=` one line per secret reference of its `credential_scopes`, from `/etc/autonomous-trading/credentials/<participant>/`, never an `EnvironmentFile=`; every non-kernel unit gets `InaccessiblePaths=-/run/credentials/<other unit>` for every other unit, `-/etc/autonomous-trading/credentials` and each `file` secret locator, and `ProtectProc=invisible`, except the observer (`ProtectProc=default`, step 5 carry-in).
* **The directive list per unit** (the render check refuses any other directive, in the unit or in an effective drop-in):

  | Units | Allowed |
  |---|---|
  | Every service | `[Unit]` `Description`, `Documentation`, `After`, `Wants`, `StartLimitIntervalSec`, `StartLimitBurst`; `[Service]` `Type`, `User`, `Group`, `SupplementaryGroups`, `WorkingDirectory`, `Environment`, `LoadCredential`, exactly one `ExecStart` (an absolute path under `__RELEASE__/venv/bin/`, no prefix), `Restart`, `RestartSec`, `TimeoutStopSec`, `UMask`, `OOMScoreAdjust`, `NoNewPrivileges=yes`, `PrivateTmp=yes`, `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=yes`, `ReadWritePaths`, `InaccessiblePaths`, `ProtectProc`; `[Install]` `WantedBy=multi-user.target` |
  | `at-strategy.service` | Plus `OOMPolicy`; no `[Install]` (its timer starts it) |
  | `at-supervisor.service` | Plus `Nice`, `IOSchedulingClass`, `MemoryAccounting`, `MemoryHigh`, `MemoryMax` |
  | `at-observer.service` | Plus `RuntimeDirectory`, `RuntimeDirectoryMode`, `MemoryAccounting`, `MemoryHigh`, `MemoryMax` (only when bound) |
  | `at-backup.service` | Plus `Nice`, `IOSchedulingClass`, `Slice`; `Type=oneshot`; no `[Install]` |
  | `at-operator.service` | The service set plus what SLICE-10 names for it, listed in `deploy/install.py` beside the others |
  | Timers | `[Unit]` `Description`; `[Timer]` `OnCalendar`, `RandomizedDelaySec`, `Persistent`, `Unit`; `[Install]` `WantedBy=timers.target` |

* **`ReadWritePaths` per unit** (everything else read-only under `ProtectSystem=strict`): the kernel: the parent of `ledger.path`, `paths.evidence`, `<paths.run>/kernel`, `<paths.run>/health`, `<paths.run>/identity`, `<paths.scratch>/kernel`; the market: the parent of `market.store_path`, `<paths.run>/health`, identity, its scratch; the supervisor: `supervisor.assessment_path`, `<paths.run>/supervisor`, identity, its scratch; the observer: identity, its scratch (its socket directory comes from `RuntimeDirectory=`); the strategy host: `<paths.evidence>/strategies`, identity, its scratch; the operator relay: `<paths.run>/operator`, identity, its scratch; cognition: `<paths.run>/cognition`, identity, its scratch; the backup: its scratch, and its staging once step 8 adds the row.
* **Per unit, keeping legacy's live values** (Fork calls 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9; the runtime spec's table): the kernel `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=60`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=900`, `StartLimitBurst=3`, `TimeoutStopSec=60`, `UMask=0027` (Fork call 9), at boot, no memory cap, `OOMScoreAdjust=-800`, no `WatchdogSec`; the market `Restart=always`, 5 s, `UMask=0027`, no cap, `-600`; the strategy host by its timer, `on-failure`, 60 s, 3 in 900 s, `OOMPolicy=stop`, `UMask=0077`, `-800`, no cap; the supervisor `always`, 10 s, `Nice=19`, idle I/O, `UMask=0027`, `MemoryHigh`/`MemoryMax` from `envelope.supervisor.*` and **legacy's `768M`/`1200M` while unbound** (`deploy/systemd/at-monitor-v2-sweep.service:22-23`), `500`, `NoNewPrivileges`; the observer `always`, 5 s, `UMask=0077`, caps from `envelope.observer.*` when bound and none while unbound (legacy's gateway had none, `deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:35-54`), **`OOMScoreAdjust=-600`, legacy's key-holding gateway's** (`deploy/unit_envelope.py:70`), `RuntimeDirectory=at-observer`, `RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750`, `Group=at-observer-readers`; the operator as SLICE-10 set it, `-400`; the backup oneshot, `Nice=10`, idle I/O, `UMask=0077`, `300`, `Slice=at.slice`, its timer 11:45 UTC, `RandomizedDelaySec=15min`, `Persistent=true`, **enabled only when the backup destination is bound** (legacy, `deploy/install-gateway.sh:1087-1092`). The kill order is written in the unit itself, not a drop-in. `status` lists each value rendered from legacy while its setting is unmeasured.
* **The render checks** (refuse the render, `CFG-R10`), run by `render`, again by `apply-root` on its own rendering, and again by `apply-root` on each effective unit after the reload: a placeholder left; a directive outside the unit's list, a second `Exec*=`, any `+`, `!`, `@` or `-` prefix on `ExecStart`, `User=root` or `Group=root`, `AmbientCapabilities=`, `EnvironmentFile=`, `PassEnvironment=` (legacy's escalation check, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:844-875`); **the `at.slice` allowlist** (step 6): only `at-backup.service`, the research units and transient `at-work-*`, `at-shadow-copier-*` may name `at.slice` or an `at-*.slice`; a `MemoryMax`/`MemoryHigh` on the kernel, market or strategy unit; the key-holder rule (below); an `AT_` name the participant may not receive, and `AT_REHEARSAL` in any unit outside `--rehearse`; a path breaking the path rules; a `ReadWritePaths=` other than the unit's row.
* **PRF-CFG-06:** for each participant, its `--check` run with exactly the environment its rendered unit sets (parsed back from the unit text) passes, and adding a forbidden credential or an unlisted `AT_` name makes it refuse.

#### Key holders (Fork call 8)

The market unit keeps the account's key, as legacy's market poller did in the key-holding gateway (`deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:40`). The project's rule is that no spawned child process holds the key, not that only two units may. The render check therefore proves two things, without reading the holder list from `PARTICIPANTS` alone (the review's item 10):

* **No other unit gets it.** `deploy/install.py` holds its own literal `KEY_HOLDER_UNITS = {"at-kernel.service", "at-market.service", "at-observer.service"}`, and a test asserts it equals the units of `_HOLDS_BROKER_KEY` (OA_NEW `settings/schema.py:465`), so a change to either fails. A unit outside it with a `LoadCredential=` of the `openalgo/api_key` reference, a `BROKER_KEY_NAMES` name anywhere, or a holder's credential directory outside `InaccessiblePaths=` refuses.
* **No unit passes it on.** Each holder's unit has exactly one `ExecStart` and no other `Exec*=` (the directive list). A test composes each holder (the kernel, the market process, the observer) with `subprocess.Popen`, `os.exec*`, `os.spawn*`, `os.posix_spawn*` and `os.system` recorded, runs one pass or call, and asserts every child's environment has no `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` and no `BROKER_KEY_NAMES` name (today the only child is `systemctl show` with `SYSTEMCTL_ENV`).

#### Root-written rules

Each is rendered from root-owned inputs by the same functions in `install-root-copy` and `apply-root`; `apply-root` refuses an existing file whose text is not byte-equal to its rendering. Sudoers files are staged in a root-owned temporary file, checked with `visudo -cqf` and by `sudoers_problems` (legacy's shape check ported, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:942-959`: comments and exactly the expected lines), and installed `0440` (`:976-1003`).

1. **`/etc/sudoers.d/autonomous-trading-apply-root`**: two lines per present copy (at most two copies), `apply-root` and `enable-units`, each with no further argument and no wildcard, so sudo matches the command exactly:
   `at-installer ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: /usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/copies/<R>/venv/bin/python -I /usr/local/lib/autonomous-trading/copies/<R>/deploy/install.py apply-root`
   and the same line ending `enable-units`. Both modes refuse unless the copy's profile sha256 equals the `profile_sha256` the owner recorded for it in `applied.jsonl`.
2. **`/etc/sudoers.d/autonomous-trading-isolated-work`**: `%at-operators ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: <running copy>/venv/bin/python -I <running copy>/deploy/isolated_work.py --work-dir *`. **Under sudo** (effective uid 0 with `SUDO_USER` set) `scripts/isolated_work.py` refuses (exit 20) `--profile` and uses only the copy's `profile/production.yaml`, after checking its sha256 against the owner's `profile_sha256` in `applied.jsonl` (the profile is pinned and owner-approved; sudo's `env_reset` already drops `AT_PROFILE` and `AT_ISOLATION_PROFILE`); `--run-as` other than `SUDO_USER`, and a `SUDO_USER` that is root or a participant's system user; `--shadow` (its own rule); a `--work-dir` that does not exist, is a symlink, is not owned by `SUDO_USER`, or is inside or contains a protected root, a hidden root, a key file, `paths.run`, `release.root`, the copies or the credentials directory; a `--unit` not named `at-work-*.service`; an `--oom` below the default 900 (the kill order, Fork call 6); a `--report` outside the work directory, which is opened `O_NOFOLLOW | O_EXCL` relative to the work directory's descriptor. The manager is the system manager, and the unit runs as `SUDO_USER`. This settles step 6's carried open item (a) and the copier carry-in.
3. **`/etc/sudoers.d/autonomous-trading-shadow-copier`**: `%at-operators ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: <running copy>/venv/bin/python -I <running copy>/deploy/isolated_work.py --shadow *`. Under sudo with `--shadow`, `--profile`, `--run-as` and `--network` refuse; step 6's rule 12 already pins the copier's arguments to the profile and its user to `shadow.legacy_user`. SLICE-13 step 6 is its user.
4. **`/etc/polkit-1/rules.d/60-autonomous-trading-supervisor.rules`**: `polkit_rule("at-supervisor", "at.slice")` (step 4).
5. **`/etc/polkit-1/rules.d/60-autonomous-trading-installer.rules`**: new `installer_polkit_rule(user, units, verbs)` in `operations/isolation.py`, rendered for `at-installer`, the exact unit list of the composition (`at-kernel.service`, `at-market.service`, `at-supervisor.service`, `at-observer.service`, `at-strategy.service`, `at-strategy.timer`, `at-operator.service`, `at-backup.service`, `at-backup.timer`) and the verbs `start`, `stop` and `reset-failed` (the re-review's G: a kernel that hit `StartLimitBurst`, 3 in 900 s, must be restartable by `restore` and `resume`): `org.freedesktop.systemd1.manage-units` is allowed only when `action.lookup("unit")` is in the list and `action.lookup("verb")` is one of the three; everything else `polkit.Result.NOT_HANDLED`. `daemon-reload` is `apply-root`'s and enabling is `enable-units`', both as root, so the installer needs no other right.

#### The one root step (`apply-root`)

`apply-root` takes no argument and applies the release its own copy names. It refuses (exit 3, `CFG-R10` unless named) unless: the effective uid is 0; it runs with `python -I`; `SUDO_USER` is `at-installer`; its copy and every ancestor are root-owned and not group- or other-writable (`root_owned`); its copy's `deploy/` matches the copy's `artifact.deploy` (`CFG-R06`); `applied.jsonl` holds the owner's `install-root-copy` record for this copy's digest; and the copy's `profile/production.yaml` has that record's `profile_sha256`. Then, in order:

1. **The release.** `<release.root>/<release_id>/`, where `release_id` is the copy's; its `bundle.json` digest must equal the copy's (`CFG-R06`).
2. **Its own rendering.** It renders the units, `sysusers.d`, the directory table, the polkit rules and the sudoers files from root-owned inputs only (its copy's templates and `PARTICIPANTS`, the copy's pinned profile) and reruns every render check, the path allowlist included.
3. **The plan as data.** It reads `<release>/units/plan.json` and refuses (`plan_differs <path>`) unless every file, target, mode, owner and sha256 equals its rendering's. The plan is never the source of what is written.
4. **Foreign files** (the review's item 5). For every target path, and every drop-in directory that applies to a rendered unit (`<unit>.d/`, each dash prefix such as `at-.service.d/`, and `service.d/` or `timer.d/`) under `/etc/systemd/system`, `/run/systemd/system`, `/usr/local/lib/systemd/system` and `/usr/lib/systemd/system`, it refuses (`foreign <path>`) when a file exists there whose sha256 a target release did not write at that path. Its evidence is its own root-owned `applied.jsonl`; the same facts go into `transitions.jsonl` as `UNITS_WRITTEN`'s facts, but `apply-root` does not trust that file, which `at-installer` can write. **So on a host where legacy's `at-strategy.service`, `at-strategy.timer`, `at-backup.*`, or the drop-ins `at-strategy.service.d/entry-limits.conf` and `tier1-authority.conf` exist, `apply-root` refuses until SLICE-14 removes them.** On a first install no target record exists, so any existing file at a target path refuses.
5. **Directories** from the directory table (above).
6. **Writes.** Each file to a temporary name in its target directory (`O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`), then a rename; each file it replaces is first copied to `/var/lib/autonomous-trading/installed/<previous release_id>/`, and the previous release's `units/installed/` (the restore set) must be byte-equal to those copies.
7. **Apply.** `systemd-sysusers` and `systemctl daemon-reload`. **It never enables and starts nothing** (the re-review's D): a unit written here starts at boot only after `enable-units` has run at `STARTED`, so a refused activation followed by a reboot starts nothing of the target.
8. **The effective units.** For each rendered unit, `systemctl cat <unit>` (the unit file with every drop-in systemd applies) must equal the rendered text alone, and the render checks run on that text. A difference refuses (`effective <unit>`) after putting back the files of step 6 and reloading.
9. **Record.** Appends each written path and sha256 with the `release_id` to `applied.jsonl` and prints the same as JSON for the installer's record.

**It never deletes a file** it did not write in the same run (step 8's put-back), and never writes outside the rendered targets and the table's directories.

#### Unit names

**Not renamed** (the review's item 5 allows a rename only if cleaner). A unit's name is a peer identity: SLICE-10's channel admits strategy peers by unit (OA_NEW `tests/conftest.py:1252`, `services: {"at-strategy.service": …}`), and SLICE-09's T-59, SLICE-13's texts and `COMPOSITIONS` (OA_NEW `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py:1114-1137`) name `at-strategy.service` and `at-backup.service`. A rename would cross four owners for no gain: the foreign-file refusal already keeps the two sets apart, and this slice never installs beside legacy (`OLD_WRITER_GONE`). The collision means the target's units cannot be written on the production host before SLICE-14 removes legacy's; step 10 only stages, and writes no unit.

#### Reading the stores

(The re-review's B; settled by design.) The ledger and the market store are WAL databases opened read-only by readers (`SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY`, OA_NEW `kernel/ledger/runtime.py:77-87`; the market store's `connect`, `market/store.py:272`). Since SQLite 3.22 such a reader opens only if the `-wal` and `-shm` files exist, or it can create them, or it opens with `immutable=1`; a writer that closes cleanly deletes both. Readers here cannot create them (the directories are `2750`), and **readers never get write access to `-shm`**: that would let any member of the reader group corrupt the authoritative ledger's WAL index.

**`read_mode(unit_state, cgroup_empty, wal_bytes) -> "immutable" | "readonly" | "unavailable"`** (new, `operations/deployment.py`, no kernel import), from the writer's unit (`at-kernel.service` for the ledger, `at-market.service` for the market store):

| Writer | Mode |
|---|---|
| **Verified gone:** unit `inactive` or `failed`, `MainPID` 0, its cgroup empty, **and no `-wal` file or a zero-length one** | `immutable`: opened as `file:<path>?immutable=1` (nothing can write it, and with no WAL content nothing committed is skipped) |
| **Running:** unit `active` with a `MainPID` | `readonly`: plain read-only, using the writer's `-wal` and `-shm` |
| **Verified gone, with a non-empty `-wal`** (a crash left committed data in it) | `copy`: the database and its `-wal` (both `0640`, readable by the reader's group) are copied into the reader's private scratch (`<paths.scratch>/<participant>/readcopy/<n>/`, `0700`), and the copy is opened normally, so SQLite replays the WAL there; **the original is never opened**; the copy is removed after the read (the third review's item 2) |
| Otherwise (starting, stopping, unit state unreadable) | `unavailable` |

`LedgerReader.open` and `MarketReader.open` gain `mode=` (`readonly` the default, `immutable` adding the URI flag; the writer's open is unchanged). `copy` is done by `open_reader(path, mode, scratch)` in `operations/deployment.py`, which also checks that the `-wal` and the database did not change while copying (size and `mtime` before and after; a change gives `unavailable`). **The supervisor** records `ledger_gap readonly` for `unavailable` and for a `readonly` open that fails (`SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT`), and guesses past neither (step 4's rule); so after a crash, including a kernel left `failed` at its start limit, it keeps reading the ledger through `copy`. **The installer** refuses (`CFG-R13 store_unreadable <store>`) on `unavailable` or a failed open. So at `BOUNDARY_QUIET` (writers running) it reads `readonly`, and at `QUIET_AFTER_STOP`, `OLD_WRITER_GONE` and `QUIET_BEFORE_SWITCH` (the kernel and market verified gone) it reads `immutable`, or `copy` after a crash, so a release that fixes a crash-looping kernel can still pass the boundary. **The kernel and the strategy host** reading the market store use the same selection on `at-market.service`; while the market process restarts their read is `unavailable` and the open raises, and what they do on a failed market read stays the market spec's, unchanged here (no new trading gate). **A test shows it is contained:** a kernel pass whose market-store open raises completes, records no `pass_fault`, and its stop legs stay as they were (the calendar read is guarded, OA_NEW `market/view.py:1009`; the test covers the kernel's other market reads too).

**Fork call 9's reason, restated:** SQLite gives `-wal` and `-shm` the database file's mode, so the ledger itself needs only its file at `0640`; `UMask=0027` is needed for the evidence files, the market store's files and the assessments, which other users read and which the code creates without a `chmod`.

**Proof.** Now: a unit test of `read_mode` over every row, and single-user opens on every platform: a WAL database closed cleanly opened `immutable`; **the crash case through `copy`** (a writer process killed with committed rows still in its `-wal`: the copy reads every committed row, and the original's bytes, `-wal` and `-shm` are unchanged); a running writer read `readonly`. The **three-case real-users check** (writer running; writer stopped cleanly; writer crashed) needs root to create the users; there is no disposable root host and the EC2 is production, so it is a row of step 10's staging, on the owner's word.

#### Effective units, every time

(The re-review's E; the third review's item 4.) **Only units `applied.jsonl` records as written by a target release are compared;** every other rendered unit is reported `not installed` (so step 10's `check` on a legacy host, where `systemctl cat at-strategy.service` shows legacy's unit, is not refused). The comparison `apply-root` makes after its reload (each `systemctl cat <unit>` equal to the rendering alone, and the render checks on that text) also runs, without root, in **`check`** (the installed units against the running release's rendering: drift refuses `CFG-R10 effective <unit>`), in **`status`** (reported per unit) and in **`CONVERGED`** (a difference leaves the operation unavailable, naming the unit). So a drop-in or a changed file appearing after `apply-root` (from legacy's `unit_envelope.py`, say) is seen at every later command.

#### `stage` (`STAGING` → `STAGED`)

* **Resources first,** before writing anything: memory available below `install.min_memory_mb`, or free disk on `release.root` below the release's size plus `storage.critical_reserve_bytes`, refuses. **While either is unbound, legacy's 1024 MB and 2048 MB apply** (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:522-543`; step 6's rule for legacy values until step 9). Not a money value.
* Creates `<release_id>/`, a virtual environment, `pip install --require-hashes --no-deps -r requirements.lock` and the wheel, keeps the wheel and lock in `artifact/`, copies `deploy/` and `scripts/isolated_work.py`, writes `bundle.json` with `build_manifest` from inside the release's interpreter (step 2), and renders `units/`. Production staging refuses (`CFG-R08`) while `release.license` or any owner-only item is unbound.
* **CP-R1:** a crash leaves a partial directory; `resume` deletes and rebuilds it; the host log keeps the attempt.

#### `check` (`VERIFIED_DISARMED`)

Every entrypoint's `--check` from the release, in a blank environment with no credential; `verify_loaded`; `artifact.deploy` against the staged `deploy/`; `compatible` and `validate_composition` against the live ledger, read-only (step 2); `CFG-R11` for an unrunnable live leg; the path allowlist; the effective-unit comparison ([above](#effective-units-every-time)); a `rehearsal:` lease store or `AT_REHEARSAL` in the profile refuses (`CFG-R08`). **The filesystem rule** (the review's item 7; step 4's carry-in): `bootstrap.check` for the supervisor refuses (`refused: supervisor.assessment_path shares a filesystem with ledger.path`) when the nearest existing ancestors of the two paths have the same `st_dev`, with the same `same_filesystem` the supervisor's start uses (OA_NEW `operations/supervisor.py:700-703`), so a release can no longer reach `VERIFIED_DISARMED` and fail only at `OBSERVERS_STARTED`. **Legacy's account-limits drop-in** (R-6, step 1 carry-in as amended): the installer reads `MCP_LIMITS__*` from `at-strategy.service.d/entry-limits.conf` when present and refuses (`CFG-R08`) when the profile's `account.limits.*` differs or is unbound. `facts` records `from_revision`/`to_revision`. **The drop-in is never removed by this slice:** removing it on a host where legacy runs would change legacy's live limits; SLICE-14's cutover removes it.

#### `activate`

The configuration spec's states, in order, each `intent` then `done`, `fsync`ed before and after its action. Stops and starts go through the installer's polkit rule.

1. **`AUTHORIZED`**: `--authorized-by`, `--instruction`, the `release_id` and whether it migrates are recorded; a missing or mismatched record refuses `CFG-R12`.
2. **`BOUNDARY_QUIET`**: `boundary(eff, now, since) -> list[str]` in `src/autonomous_trading/operations/activation.py` (new, T-105), every reason refusing `CFG-R13 boundary_not_quiet`, for each bound account:
   * **the calendar** from the market store's calendar port (`market/view.py:985`), the store opened in the mode `read_mode` selects: `Holiday`, or a `TradingDay` whose `close_time` is past, is closed; a `DayUnavailable` refuses (unknown refuses, legacy's `src/tier1/cutover.py:171-172`), and so does a `TradingDay` not yet closed;
   * **the broker, through the supervisor's latest assessment** (the review's item 4; no new read on the account): the last line of `<supervisor.assessment_path>/index/<day>.jsonl` and its object, read as `at-installer` through `at-assessment-readers`. It is fresh only when it was published after this state's `intent` record (`since`) and each book it used has `as_of` within `reconciliation.max_observation_age_secs` of now (the kernel's own freshness rule, already shared with the supervisor; no new value). The installer waits for a fresh one at most that long, then refuses `broker_unobserved`. An `observation_gap` verdict for the account refuses; so does a non-zero `broker.open_positions` or `broker.working_orders` (`:173-181`). **The assessment gains `broker`:** `{account: {"open_positions": n, "working_orders": n}}`, counted by the supervisor from the positions and orderbook it already reads, by legacy's rule (`quantity` not 0; status one of the five words), a change to `operations/supervisor.py` in this step;
   * **the ledger** through `LedgerReader` (step 4), in the mode [`read_mode`](#reading-the-stores) selects (`store_unreadable` when it is `unavailable`): an unresolved attempt, a command `IN_FLIGHT`, `UNCERTAIN` or `WORKING`, or a held reservation refuses (`:186-202`);
   * **a legacy sender** (the review's item 6): any running legacy unit that sends (`at-tier1-authority.service`, `mcp-gateway*.service`) refuses here, before anything stops (`:203-205`).
3. **`STOPPING` → `STOPPED`**: the actors only, in legacy's order (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:585-594`): timers, the strategy host, the operator relay, the market process, the kernel last. The stopped set is written before each stop. The observers keep running.
4. **`QUIET_AFTER_STOP`**: `boundary` again.
5. **`OLD_WRITER_GONE`**: the old kernel unit is `inactive` with `MainPID=0` and no process left in its cgroup; each bound account's lease reads released `clean` by the old epoch (`LeaseStore.read`, `kernel/recovery.py:38-43`); no legacy sender runs (again).
6. **`UNITS_WRITTEN`**: `sudo` `apply-root` from the target's copy; nothing started.
7. **`QUIET_BEFORE_SWITCH`**: `boundary` once more (`:1424-1425`).
8. **`SWITCHED`**: the old observers are stopped; `current` is replaced by one `rename(2)` of a new link over the old.
9. **`OBSERVERS_STARTED`**, **`MARKET_STARTED`**: each started and verified before the next: its unit `active`, its identity record (`_write_identity`) owned by its participant's user, from its own `MainPID`, naming this `bundle_digest` and its `settings.revision` (legacy's PID match, `:1321-1348`, retried every 3 s for 60 s, `:521`), its handshake passed; the supervisor has published one assessment.
10. **`MIGRATION_PENDING`** (only when the release migrates), then **`KERNEL_STARTED`**: `facts` records the stored schema revision after recovery, and `MIGRATED` when it rose.
11. **`STARTED`**: `sudo` `enable-units` from the target's copy (the first moment anything of the target is enabled); then the strategy host's timer, the operator relay, the backup timer (when its destination is bound).
12. **`CONVERGED`**, per operation (trading, protection, observation, owner commands, backup): each long-running required participant's identity record matches its `MainPID`, owner, bundle and revision; the kernel recorded `RELEASE_BOUND` with this bundle; each timer `enabled` and waiting; each unit's effective text equals the rendering. `facts` lists each operation available or not, naming the missing participant.

**A refusal, by where it happens** (the review's item 6; each step recorded as a fact of `FAILED`, and the end state is always the previous release running):

| Refused at or after | Before | Then |
|---|---|---|
| `AUTHORIZED`, `BOUNDARY_QUIET` | `STOPPING` `intent` | Nothing to undo; `FAILED` |
| `STOPPING` `intent` (a failed stop, `QUIET_AFTER_STOP`, `OLD_WRITER_GONE`) | `UNITS_WRITTEN` `intent` | Restart the recorded stopped set on the unchanged release, verify each `active` (legacy's `restore_after_flat`, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:1471-1499`); `FAILED` |
| `UNITS_WRITTEN` `intent` (a refused or failed `apply-root`, `QUIET_BEFORE_SWITCH`) | `SWITCHED` `done` | First `apply-root` from the **running** release's copy, which re-renders the previous set; verify each file byte-equal to the previous release's `units/installed/` and each `systemctl cat` equal to it; reload; only then restart the stopped set; `FAILED` |
| `SWITCHED` `done` | — | `restore` (below) |

**First install** (no `current`, no copy running): the target's observers are the only route to the broker, and they send nothing, so they start first: `AUTHORIZED`, `UNITS_WRITTEN`, `SWITCHED`, `OBSERVERS_STARTED`, then `BOUNDARY_QUIET` and `OLD_WRITER_GONE` (legacy senders); the stop states are recorded `skipped: no running release`. A refusal stops the observers and ends `FAILED`; nothing that sends has started, and **nothing is enabled** (`enable-units` runs only at `STARTED`), so a reboot after the refusal starts nothing of the target.

#### `resume` and `restore`

* **`resume`** reads the last record and re-reads the facts (which release `current` names, which units run and from which release's unit file, which identity records exist, the stored schema revision), never inferring success from the link or a process. It continues from the first state whose facts are not met, or, after a refusal, finishes the refusal's row above. CP-R1…CP-R7 as the configuration spec's table. A reboot between `UNITS_WRITTEN` and `SWITCHED` starts the old release under new unit files; `resume` finds the units running from a release other than `current`'s unit set and finishes the `UNITS_WRITTEN` row.
* **`restore`**: (1) stop every unit of the new set and verify each `inactive` with no process; (2) `apply-root` from the previous release's copy, which re-renders the old set, checked byte-equal to `units/installed/`; reload; put back the old link; (3) start the old set: observers, market, kernel, the rest (`reset-failed` first for any unit that hit its start limit); `enable-units` from the previous copy; judge convergence. After `MIGRATED`, refused unless the old bundle is still `compatible` (`CFG-R09`). **No step starts a unit of one set while a unit of the other runs** (a check before every start).

#### The rehearsal composition

(The review's item 9.) Loops need a production profile, and `lease_store_for` always raises in this release (OA_NEW `bootstrap.py:984-986`), so the rehearsal has its own composition, usable only under `--rehearse`:

* **`install.py --rehearse DIR --init`** writes `DIR/.rehearsal.json` (`{"format": "at-rehearsal/1", "nonce": <32 hex>}`) and a production-kind rehearsal profile with `recovery.lease_store: rehearsal:DIR/leases`, `openalgo.base_url: http://127.0.0.1:9` (a closed loopback port), the `openalgo/api_key` reference a `file` locator to `DIR/keys/synthetic` holding a synthetic value that is no key, and a market store seeded from a fixture (the market process's own reads go to the closed URL, fail and are recorded; the calendar comes from the seeded store). Every rehearsal unit's environment adds `AT_REHEARSAL=DIR:<nonce>`.
* **`adapters/rehearsal.py`** (new, T-108): `RehearsalLeaseStore` (SLICE-03's fake lease store over files in `DIR/leases`, under `flock`) and `RehearsalBroker` (the `SimulatedBroker` with its books in `DIR/broker.json` under `flock`, so the kernel's order port and the observer's books read one state, and a test seeds or changes positions and orders). Both construct only through `rehearsal_guard(environ, base_url)`: `AT_REHEARSAL` set, its directory holding `.rehearsal.json` with the same nonce, `base_url` resolving to loopback, and no `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY`; otherwise `RefusingParameterUnbound`, as today. `lease_store_for` accepts the `rehearsal:` scheme only through it; the kernel's port factory (`bootstrap._compose_production_kernel`) and `compose_observer`'s books use `RehearsalBroker` only through it; otherwise both are unchanged.
* **The fences:** no rehearsal unit gets `LoadCredential=` or `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY`; the render check refuses `AT_REHEARSAL` outside `--rehearse`; `check` refuses a `rehearsal:` lease store; the real `apply-root` refuses a plan naming either. **On Linux** `fake_systemctl` starts each unit as a transient unit of the caller's user manager (`systemd-run --user --unit=at-rh-<nonce[:8]>-<unit>`, the rendered environment, `PrivateNetwork=yes`), so `systemctl --user show` is real and each child sees only `lo`; `SystemctlProbe` and the main-pid lookups the kernel and observer pass to their channel servers take a `--user` flag and the unit prefix only under `AT_REHEARSAL`. **Elsewhere** it runs child process groups (legacy's model), and the closed URL is the only network fence, as the report records.
* **What a rehearsal unit applies** (the re-review's F), listed in each rehearsal's report: of the rendered directives, `Environment=` (plus `AT_REHEARSAL`), `ExecStart=`, `Restart=`, `RestartSec=`, `StartLimit*`, `TimeoutStopSec=`, `UMask=`, `NoNewPrivileges=yes`, `PrivateTmp=yes`, `ProtectSystem=strict` with `ReadWritePaths=` inside `DIR`, and `InaccessiblePaths=` over the credential roots and key files; **not** `User=`, `Group=`, `SupplementaryGroups=`, `LoadCredential=` (the caller's user manager runs them as the caller, with no credential) or `ProtectHome=` (`DIR` is under the caller's home); and in place of the unit's own memory and kill values, **step 6's per-run caps**: `MemoryMax=1500M`, `MemorySwapMax=0`, `CPUQuota` of half the CPUs, `Nice=19`, `IOSchedulingClass=idle`, `OOMScoreAdjust=1000`. Every rehearsal path is inside `DIR`; `--rehearse` refuses any other.
* **On the EC2**, rehearsal rows run only outside session hours (the runner refuses between 09:00 and 15:45 IST on a trading day, or when the calendar cannot say), in a throwaway `DIR` under `~/claude-desktop/outputs`, under `--rehearse`, removed afterwards. **The rows that start production-profile processes there** (the frozen kernel, the real `systemctl show`, `SIGTERM`, the network fence) **need the owner's word**; without it they run on a disposable host.

#### `config/live.example.yaml` and documents

* **`config/live.example.yaml`** completed, with every refusing field `null` and a comment naming its register row: `profile: production`; the `interfaces.*` session values; every Q-answer binding; `policies` carrying legacy's bound values (step 3 carry-in); **the drop-in's nine account limits** (R-5, legacy's live values `deploy/systemd/at-strategy-entry-limits.conf:3-11`); the MCP profiles' `process` sections with `{"mcp:*": ["*"]}` and only the read-only profile enabled; the console's `OWNER_API_URL`, `MCP_GATEWAY_URL`, uid and headers; `operator.playbooks`; `isolation.key_files` naming legacy's key files and `isolation.hidden_roots` legacy's home; the paths of the directory table, none under `/home`. No credential.
* **`docs/operations.md`**: releases, the root copy and the owner's digest check, resume, restore, the activation boundary, the owner-only items, the sudoers and polkit rules, the directory table, and keeping OpenAlgo's access log from recording query strings. **`docs/decisions.md`**: each Fork call of this slice.

#### Fork calls in this step

* **1. When a release may be activated:** legacy's list at three points, plus `OLD_WRITER_GONE` (the slice's table); legacy senders checked at `BOUNDARY_QUIET` and `OLD_WRITER_GONE`.
* **2, 3, 4, 6, 8:** rendered with legacy's values (the slice's table): pass cadence settings, restart 60 s and 3 in 900 s, no watchdog, uncapped trading units with legacy's kill order, the market process holding the account's key.
* **9. `UMask`** (new, the slice's table): the kernel `0027` where legacy's authority had `0077`; the market and supervisor `0027` where legacy's gateway and monitor had systemd's default. The ledger itself would need only its file at `0640` (SQLite copies its mode to `-wal` and `-shm`); the umask is for the evidence files, the market store and the assessments other users read. **For the owner's review.**
* **Unbound watcher values:** the supervisor's `768M`/`1200M` and the observer's `-600` and no cap, legacy's, until step 9 measures them.
* **Staging resources:** legacy's 1024 MB and 2048 MB until step 9 binds them. Not money.

#### Deviations in this step

* **A release directory and one link,** not a checkout and `pip install -e` (the slice's Deviation).
* **Resume from facts:** legacy has none; a rerun started over (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:1401-1469`).
* **Per-unit users, `LoadCredential=` and no shared settings file** (the slice's Deviation), with the directory table and `ProtectSystem=strict`, `ProtectHome=yes` on every unit (legacy: only the gateway, `deploy/systemd/mcp-gateway.service:52-54`).
* **Root runs only a root-owned, owner-approved copy, through exact sudoers lines and two polkit rules;** legacy's app user could stop, restart, enable or disable any `at-*` or `mcp-gateway*` service through sudo (`deploy/deploy_tier1.py:955-956`).
* **First install starts the observers before the boundary** (they send nothing).
* **No `--accept-unsettled`** (the slice's Deviation).
* **The kill order lives in the unit,** not in a `51-oom.conf` drop-in.

#### Carried to later slices

* **SLICE-14:** a root operation of its own that removes legacy's units and drop-ins (`at-strategy.service.d/entry-limits.conf`, `tier1-authority.conf`, the `50-envelope.conf` and `51-oom.conf` drop-ins, legacy's `at-strategy.*`, `at-backup.*`), **legacy's sudoers rules** (`/etc/sudoers.d/at-session-reset`, `at-monitor-v2-cutover`, whose shape lets the app user stop, restart, enable or disable any `at-*` service, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:942-959`), **legacy's writers that act by name** (its `at` command and `deploy/unit_envelope.py`'s drop-in installer) and **legacy's `.wants/` enablement links** for the reused names, keeping a byte copy of each removed file so legacy can be restored; recorded in `applied.jsonl` so `apply-root` then accepts the paths. `apply-root` never deletes. Until SLICE-14, the effective-unit comparison at `check`, `status` and `CONVERGED` catches any drift.
* **SLICE-10:** the `at` commands carried from step 1 (`install`, `update`, `restart`, `deploy`, `config`, `approver`), as thin wrappers over `deploy/install.py`; OA_NEW `__main__.py` has none.
* **SLICE-09:** T-59 keeps the name `at-strategy.service`; its state directory joins the directory table.
* **SLICE-12:** the research units' names (`at-research-worker.service`, `at-research-model.service`, `at-research.slice`) collide with legacy's in the same way; `apply-root` refuses them on a legacy host until SLICE-14.

#### Open for review

* **(a) Settled:** the read modes ([Reading the stores](#reading-the-stores)); readers never write `-shm`. Its real-users proof is step 10's, on the owner's word.
* **(b) The root rows** (real users, the directory table, `apply-root`, `enable-units`, the two-uid case, `pkcheck`, the three store cases, kill release across users) run in step 10's staging on the owner's word: there is no disposable root host, and the EC2 is production.
* **(c) The EC2 runs systemd 257 (Debian 13)** (step 6 as built): `LoadCredential=`, `ProtectProc=` and `PrivatePIDs=` are available; step 10 records it formally.

### Tests

**`tests/contracts/test_rendered_units.py`** (T-77; PRF-CFG-06, PRF-OPS-07):

| Group | Cases |
|---|---|
| From the contract | Every unit-bearing participant renders; `User=`, `Group=` (the observer's `at-observer-readers`), `SupplementaryGroups=` as `PARTICIPANTS`; the kernel in `at-channel`; `at-installer` with its four groups; `Environment=` only the allowed names; `LoadCredential=` only its scopes' references; no `EnvironmentFile=` anywhere |
| Directives | Each unit's directives (and each effective drop-in's) within its list; exactly one `ExecStart`, no prefix; `ReadWritePaths=` equal to the unit's row |
| Directory table | The rendered table equals the expected rows for a test profile, the strategy store, health, operator, cognition and kill rows included; identity and health sticky, the kill directory not; the operator not in `at-operators`, the kernel in it; **the allowlist:** a path outside the three roots, a relative path, a component ending `.d`, one containing `systemd`, `sudoers` or `polkit`, or a row inside `release.root` → `CFG-R10`; under `--rehearse`, a path outside `DIR` → `CFG-R10` |
| Credentials | Every non-kernel unit has `InaccessiblePaths=` over every other unit's credential directory and the credentials root, and `ProtectProc=invisible` (the observer `default`) |
| Key holders | `KEY_HOLDER_UNITS` equals the units of `_HOLDS_BROKER_KEY`; no other unit names the key or a holder's credential; holders have one `Exec*`; each holder's composition starts no child with `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` or a key name |
| Legacy's values | Each unit's restart, start mode, stop timeout, `UMask`, kill order and caps as the table; the kernel, market and strategy units carry no `MemoryMax` or `MemoryHigh` whatever the settings; the supervisor `768M`/`1200M` while unbound and the bound values when bound; the observer `-600` and no cap while unbound |
| Render checks | A leftover placeholder, an extra directive, a second `Exec*`, a `+` prefix, `User=root`, an `EnvironmentFile=`, a trading unit naming `at.slice` or `at-x.slice`, a forbidden credential, an unlisted `AT_` name, `AT_REHEARSAL` outside `--rehearse` → each refused alone |
| `--check` with the rendered environment | Each participant's `--check` passes with exactly its unit's environment; plus one forbidden credential, or one unlisted `AT_` name → refused |
| Root files | `sysusers.d` names one user per unit, `at-installer` and the seven groups with `PARTICIPANTS`' memberships; the supervisor's polkit rule is `polkit_rule("at-supervisor", "at.slice")`; the installer's rule allows each listed unit with `start`, `stop` and `reset-failed` and nothing else; the `apply-root` sudoers file has exactly two lines per copy, `apply-root` and `enable-units`; the three sudoers files equal their expected text, the `apply-root` line has no argument and no wildcard, every path is under the copies, and each passes `visudo -cqf` (Linux with `visudo`, else skipped); `sudoers_problems` refuses an extra line, a wildcard after `apply-root` and a path under `release.root` |
| Linux, real users (root on a disposable host, or step 10's staging on the owner's word) | **The stores (three cases):** writer running → `readonly` opens; writer stopped cleanly and verified gone → `immutable` opens; writer killed with a non-empty `-wal` → `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT` for a plain reader until recovery, and `unavailable` from `read_mode`; no reader can write `-shm`; **the strategy store:** `at-strategy` creates `<paths.evidence>/strategies/<id>/host.sqlite`; **the market heartbeat:** `at-market` writes `health/market.json` and cannot replace `health/kernel.json`; **the operator CLI socket:** `at-operator`, not a member of `at-operators`, serves it with group `at-operators` from the setgid directory, a human in `at-operators` connects, `at-market` cannot; **kill files:** a kill engaged by one member of `at-operators` is released by another, and the kernel sees both; **cognition's socket** (when composed): `at-strategy` connects, `at-market` cannot; each non-kernel unit, under its own user, cannot read the kernel's credential or another process's environment; **the channel:** `at-strategy` connects to the kernel's socket, `at-market` is refused; **the ledger:** `at-supervisor` and `at-installer` read the ledger while `at-kernel` writes it, `at-market` cannot open it; `at-installer` reads the assessment, identity records and heartbeat; the two-uid supervisor and observer case: four reads answered, the supervisor cannot read the observer's credential, `at-market` cannot connect to the observer's socket (PRF-DOM-06's Linux unit half, PRF-PRT-14's Linux half); `pkcheck` for the installer's rule: `start`, `stop` and `reset-failed` of `at-kernel.service` allowed; `restart`, and `start` of another unit, not |

**`tests/recovery/test_release_activation.py`** (T-74; PRF-CFG-03, -05, -07, -08), all in `--rehearse` directories:

| Group | Cases |
|---|---|
| A profile edit (PRF-CFG-03) | Edited under a running kernel subprocess: its heartbeat `settings_revision` and `RELEASE_BOUND` unchanged; a restart picks the new value |
| Crash points | `AT_REHEARSAL_CRASH` at each of CP-R1…CP-R7 kills the installer; `resume` reaches `CONVERGED` from the facts; `restore` returns to the old release where the table allows it; a deleted release directory leaves `transitions.jsonl` intact; the hook outside `--rehearse` → exit 2 |
| Restore | After `SWITCHED`: every new unit stopped and verified with no process before any old unit starts; the old unit set back, byte-equal to `units/installed/`; after `MIGRATED` → `CFG-R09` |
| Kill files (every platform) | In a directory with mode `2770`, a kill file made by another process is removed by `at kill-switch off` (OA_NEW `interfaces/cli.py:132-138`), and the kernel then releases |
| Read modes (every platform) | `read_mode` over each row of its table; a WAL ledger closed cleanly opens `immutable`; with a non-empty `-wal` and the writer gone → `unavailable`, the supervisor records `ledger_gap readonly`, the installer refuses `store_unreadable`; a running writer read `readonly` |
| Enabling (the re-review's D) | `apply-root` enables nothing; `enable-units` runs only at `STARTED`; **a first install refused at `BOUNDARY_QUIET`, then a simulated reboot** (the fake `systemctl` starts every enabled unit): nothing of the target starts; `enable-units` disables a unit it enabled before that the new rendering lacks |
| Effective units | A drop-in added after `apply-root` (in `<unit>.d/` or `/run/systemd/system`) → `check` refuses `CFG-R10 effective`, `status` names it, `CONVERGED` leaves the operation unavailable |
| Refusal recovery | A refusal at `QUIET_BEFORE_SWITCH`: the unit files back byte-equal to `units/installed/` and each `systemctl cat` equal, the reload recorded before the first restart, the stopped set `active` on the old unit files; a refusal at `OLD_WRITER_GONE` or after a failed stop: the stopped set restarted; an `apply-root` refused at its effective-unit check: its files put back |
| Order | The observers start and publish (identity, handshake, one assessment) before the market process and the kernel; the actors stop timers first, the kernel last; at no instant do units of two releases run together (the fake `systemctl` asserts it); a first install starts the observers before `BOUNDARY_QUIET` and nothing that sends before `CONVERGED`'s start states |
| Convergence | `CONVERGED` with the backup timer enabled and its service idle; each operation's availability; a participant whose identity record names another bundle, a stale pid, or is owned by another user → not converged |
| Rollback (PRF-CFG-05) | Refused for an older migration revision and for an unread `(kind, version)`; allowed for a compatible older bundle; no database replaced; a staging failure at each step leaves `current` and the running units unchanged |
| Composition (PRF-CFG-07) | The first-release composition with every deferred participant absent activates; removing a required one → `CFG-R10`; an optional one → activates; an unrunnable live leg → `CFG-R11` |
| Boundary (PRF-CFG-08) | No authorization → `CFG-R12`; each alone → `CFG-R13`: the calendar unknown, a trading day not closed, an open position, a working order, an unresolved attempt, a command `IN_FLIGHT`/`UNCERTAIN`/`WORKING`, a held reservation; **through the assessment:** one published before the state's `intent`, a book older than `reconciliation.max_observation_age_secs`, an `observation_gap` verdict, none within the wait (`broker_unobserved`); a legacy sender unit active → refused at `BOUNDARY_QUIET` with nothing stopped; a position appearing during the stop → refused at `QUIET_AFTER_STOP` and the stopped set restarted on the old release; the old kernel still running, a lease not released `clean`, or a legacy sender started during the stop → refused at `OLD_WRITER_GONE`; a position before the switch → `QUIET_BEFORE_SWITCH`; an unbound owner-only item → `CFG-R08` |
| Assessment's broker counts | The supervisor's `broker` block: a position with `quantity` 0 not counted, -50 counted; each of the five working statuses counted, `complete` not |
| Staging | Memory or disk below the bound values, or legacy's 1024 MB/2048 MB while unbound → refused before anything is written; a `deploy/` file changed after staging → `CFG-R06` at `check`; the legacy drop-in with a differing value → `CFG-R08` at `check`, and the drop-in file untouched; **the filesystem rule:** `supervisor.assessment_path` on the ledger's filesystem → refused at `check` (a patched `st_dev` off Linux) |
| `apply-root` | Refuses: not root, not `-I`, `SUDO_USER` other than `at-installer`, a copy not root-owned (Linux root), no `install-root-copy` record, a copy's `deploy/` changed, a copy's profile whose sha256 differs from the recorded `profile_sha256`, a profile path the allowlist refuses; a plan that differs from its own rendering in one file; a foreign unit file at a target path, a foreign drop-in in `<unit>.d/`, in `at-.service.d/` and in `/run/systemd/system`; an existing sudoers or polkit file with other text; a directory with the wrong owner or mode, or a symlink; an effective unit with an extra drop-in after the reload. Writes nothing on refusal (its put-back after the effective check included); starts nothing on success; records each written path in `applied.jsonl` |
| `install-root-copy` | Refused, with nothing built: a digest other than the bundle's; a wheel, a `requirements.lock` (an added `--index-url` line) or a profile whose sha256 differs from `artifact` or `P`; a lock line resolving only to a source distribution (`--only-binary :all:`); a copy failing `verify_loaded` is removed. The copy root-owned and `go-w`; only two copies kept; the sudoers lines name the copies present; `applied.jsonl` holds `profile_sha256`; its `install-root-copy` path runs with `autonomous_trading` unimportable (the first-install mode); its standard-library digest equals `bundle_digest` |
| Rehearsal fences | Each Linux rehearsal unit carries step 6's caps (`MemoryMax=1500M`, `MemorySwapMax=0`, half the CPUs, `Nice=19`, `OOMScoreAdjust=1000`), read back with `systemctl --user show`, and the report lists the directives applied and not; the EC2 runner refuses inside session hours; no rehearsal child's environment has `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` (every platform, from `fake_systemctl`'s recorded environments); on Linux each child's `/sys/class/net` is `lo` only; `RehearsalLeaseStore` and `RehearsalBroker` refuse without `AT_REHEARSAL`, with a wrong nonce, with a non-loopback base URL and with `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` set |
| Frozen kernel (Linux; PRF-OPS-03, PRF-PRT-06's frozen half, carried from steps 3 and 4) | An open position in `RehearsalBroker`; the kernel unit's `MainPID` gets `SIGSTOP`; the next assessments give `kernel_stalled`, the stop `broker_held`, the exits `runtime_dependent (not running)`; the supervisor's recorded calls are only `systemctl show` and no signal or stop of the kernel; after `SIGCONT`, `kernel_ok` |
| Real `systemctl show` (Linux; step 4 carry-in) | `SystemctlProbe` against the transient kernel unit: `ActiveState`, `SubState` and `MainPID` equal the unit's process; after `systemctl --user stop`, `inactive` and `MainPID` 0, so `kernel_down` |
| `SIGTERM` (Linux; step 3 carry-in) | `systemctl --user stop` of the kernel unit: exit 0, the heartbeat `stopped` with `exit_code` 0, each lease released `clean` in `RehearsalLeaseStore` |

**`tests/isolation/test_resource_storage_scope.py`**, new group **Under sudo** (effective uid 0 and `SUDO_USER` faked through the module's `system` seam; the real `sudo` rows on the disposable host): `--profile` → 20; `--run-as` another user → 20; `SUDO_USER` a participant's user → 20; `--shadow` through the general rule → 20; a work directory inside a protected root, a symlink, or not owned by `SUDO_USER` → 20; `--unit at-kernel.service` → 20; `--oom 100` → 20; `--report` outside the work directory → 20; a valid call → the unit runs as `SUDO_USER` under the copy's pinned profile.

### Completion conditions (step 7)

* The new tests and every test file importing a changed module, plus the layering and import guards, pass twice on Windows (Linux-only rows skipped with their reason). No full suite.
* The rehearsal rows that need Linux and no root (the frozen kernel, the real `systemctl show`, `SIGTERM`, the rehearsal fences) start production-profile processes, so they run on the EC2 **only on the owner's word**, outside session hours, in a throwaway `DIR` under `~/claude-desktop/outputs`, user manager only, with step 6's caps, removed afterwards; otherwise "not run: owner's word". The rows that need root (real system users, the directory table, the three store cases, kill release across users, `apply-root`, `enable-units`, `install-root-copy`, `visudo`, `pkcheck`, the real `sudo` rows, the two-uid case) are step 10's staging rows on the owner's word, recorded "not run: owner's word" here.
* Every break check fails a listed test; every file restored (md5).
* PRF-CFG-03, -05, -06, -07, -08 and PRF-OPS-07, and PRF-OPS-03's and PRF-PRT-06's frozen-kernel rows, pass in the rehearsal. **Nothing is installed on any real host, and nothing on the production host;** step 10 stages there with the owner's word.

### Step 7 as built

**Built, in part,** in OA_NEW `95aea41` on `slice-08` (from `e8bb19a`), one commit. The third review ("yes after fixes") was applied to the text above first: the off-host build record (item 1), the `copy` read mode (item 2), the non-sticky kill directory and the operator outside `at-operators` (item 3), and the effective-unit comparison limited to target-written units (item 4). Legacy at 445eb116 confirmed for item 3: every unit ran as the one app user (`deploy/systemd/*`, `User=__APP_USER__`) and `at kill-switch on` wrote `run/KILL` as that user (`deploy/at:4239-4262`), so any legacy process could remove it.

**Run.** Windows, Python 3.14 (`.venv-slice01`), plain `pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider`, affected only: the 49 test files importing a changed module, plus the guards (`test_market_windows.py`, `test_promotion_gate.py`, `test_unix_channel.py`, `test_installed_artifact.py`). Run 1: 2,015 passed, 22 skipped, 2 failed, both from the new `--check` filesystem rule over test profiles whose ledger and assessments share one temp filesystem (fixed below). After the fix, twice: 2,018 passed and 22 skipped; 2,018 passed and 22 skipped again. **Linux, on the EC2**, once, at 16:55 IST (outside 09:00–15:45), user manager only (`systemd-run --user --scope`, `MemoryMax=1G`, no swap, `CPUQuota=50%`, `nice 19`, idle I/O, under legacy's test lock), in a throwaway `~/claude-desktop/outputs/s11e7` with its own Python 3.13 venv from `requirements.lock`, removed afterwards: `test_rendered_units.py` (the `visudo -cqf` row included, not skipped), `test_release_activation.py`, the kill-file and `CANTINIT` cases: **85 passed, none skipped**. No sudo, no system manager, no production profile. `ast.parse` passes on every edited Python file.

**Break checks** (`s11f_breaks.py` in the session scratchpad): 35, each failing its listed test; every file restored (md5). B29 (sudoers accepting a command outside the copies) first passed, because the line comparison also caught it; a case with the text equal to its expected text outside the copies was added, and it then failed as required.

**Differences from the text above**, each smaller than a decision:

| Text | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `boundary`'s working orders: legacy's five words | The adapter maps `open`, `pending`, `trigger pending` to `open`, and `validation pending`, `put order req received` (and any other word) to `unknown`; both count as working | Never fewer than legacy's count; it gates activation only, not trading |
| `ledger` reason: `IN_FLIGHT`, `UNCERTAIN`, `WORKING` | As written; an `ADMITTED` command does not refuse (a test pins it) | The text's list |
| `immutable` opens check `journal_mode` | SQLite reports `delete` under `immutable=1`, so the reader checks the file header's WAL bytes (18, 19 = 2) instead | Probed: the existing `wal` check would refuse every immutable open |
| — | Probed and tested: after a crash, `immutable` misses the rows in the `-wal`; `copy` reads them and leaves the original's bytes, `-wal` and `-shm` unchanged | The third review's item 2, confirmed |
| `refusal_actions` from the `intent` records | From the furthest state reached (any record of it or a later state) | A host log may hold `done` without an earlier `intent` after a crash |
| `--check` with a test profile | `test_check_opens_nothing` patches `same_filesystem`; the installed-artifact test expects `at-supervisor --check` to refuse on exactly the filesystem rule (one temp filesystem) | The rule has its own test, `test_check_repeats_the_filesystem_rule` |
| The strategy timer's start time | `render --session-start-utc HH:MM` (legacy's session clock, `deploy/deploy_tier1.py:783-799`); unbound leaves the placeholder, which refuses the render | Legacy's timer reads the same value |
| The backup's command | `python -m autonomous_trading.operations.backup scheduled`; the module is step 8's, and the timer stays disabled until the destination is bound | Step 8 builds it |
| The kill directory `2770` | The kernel joins `at-operators`, because finding the kill file needs search permission on that directory | The third review's item 3, as ruled; recorded for the owner's review |

| 3 | `fake_systemctl` (legacy's verbs plus `cat` with drop-ins, `is-enabled`, process groups, only the unit's environment plus `AT_REHEARSAL`, never `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY`, units of two releases refused and recorded); `--rehearse DIR --init`; `RehearsalLeaseStore`, `RehearsalBroker`, `rehearsal_guard` (T-108); `lease_store_for`'s `rehearsal:` scheme, the kernel's port factory and `compose_observer`'s books only through the guard; `user_manager_argv` with step 6's caps | `deploy/install.py`, `adapters/rehearsal.py` (new), `bootstrap.py`, `operations/observer.py` |
| 4 | `activate` through `CONVERGED` (both orders), the refusal rows, `resume` (from the facts, a reboot between `UNITS_WRITTEN` and `SWITCHED`), `restore`, `status`, the crash hook (`SIGKILL`; exit 2 outside `--rehearse`) | `deploy/install.py` |
