| The owner's rule | The kernel only reads the kill directory: a test that every kill read leaves the directory and file unchanged and that no kernel module (nor `bootstrap`) creates or removes a file in a function that names the kill inputs | `tests/integration/test_kill_inputs.py` |

Tests: the rehearsal group of `tests/recovery/test_release_activation.py` (30 rows, stand-in participants on every platform; built in a file of its own and merged into T-74's file unchanged before the commit), new cases in `tests/contracts/test_rendered_units.py`, `tests/contracts/test_capabilities.py`, `tests/isolation/test_resource_storage_scope.py` (the under-sudo group) and `tests/integration/test_kill_inputs.py`.

**Run.** Windows, Python 3.14 (`.venv-slice01`), affected only: the 38 test files importing a changed module, plus the guards (`test_market_windows.py`, `test_promotion_gate.py`, `test_unix_channel.py`, `test_installed_artifact.py`, `test_secret_references.py`, `test_settings_resolver.py`), 39 files: run 1 (before the last tightened tests) 1,407 passed and 22 skipped; run 2, 1,409 passed and 22 skipped; run 3, 1,408 passed, 22 skipped and 1 failed, `test_cognition_assembly.py::test_capacity_with_threads`, a 10-second wall-clock wait that ran while a second suite shared the machine; alone it passed (its file: 78 passed, 2 skipped), and this commit changes no cognition code. The rehearsal group after its merge into T-74's file: 70 passed. **Linux, on the EC2**, once, at 18:20 IST (outside 09:00–15:45), user manager only (`systemd-run --user --scope`, `MemoryMax=1G`, no swap, `CPUQuota=50%`, `nice 19`, idle I/O, `CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY` unset, no other pytest running), in a throwaway `~/claude-desktop/outputs/s11e7b` with its own Python 3.13 venv from `requirements.lock`, removed afterwards, no process left: the rehearsal group, `test_rendered_units.py` (the `visudo -cqf` row ran) and `test_kill_inputs.py`: **178 passed, none skipped**. The first Linux run refused 24 rows on the real 2048 MB staging floor (the EC2's `/tmp` had 489 MB free); the fixture now fakes the host's resources, whose own row stays. No sudo, no system manager, no production profile. `ast.parse` passes on every edited Python file.

**Break checks** (`brk.py` with `b_step7.json`, `b_kill.json`, `b_envloc.json` in the session scratchpad): 29 for part 2, each failing its listed test, plus the kill-directory check; every file restored (md5). Three first passed and their tests were tightened: the under-sudo work directory (the step 6 admission already refused a protected root, so the row now uses `release.root`, which only the new rule guards), the legacy sender (the refusal at `OLD_WRITER_GONE` restarted everything, so the row now asserts nothing stopped), and the guard on `RehearsalLeaseStore` (the first edit was unreachable).

**Differences from the text above**, each smaller than a decision:

| Text | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `install-root-copy` and `apply-root`: the copy's bundle digest equals the staged one | Compared less `distribution.record_sha256` and each dependency's `sha256` (`location_free`) | Each RECORD lists the console scripts, whose first line names the venv's interpreter, so the full digest differs between `release.root/R/venv` and the copy's venv (checked: RECORD lists `../../Scripts/*.exe` with hashes). The wheel's and the lock's sha256 stay in both |
| `release_id` is `<UTC stamp>-<bundle_digest[:12]>` | `<UTC stamp>-<wheel sha256[:12]>` | The bundle is written inside the release's venv, after its directory is named |
| `apply-root` writes the sudoers files | Only `install-root-copy` writes them; `apply-root` refuses unless each equals the last `install-root-copy`'s rendering and recorded sha256 | The copies and the running copy change only there (open item d); `restore` re-renders an older release |
| The units' `AT_PROFILE` | `<release.root>/current/profile/production.yaml`, the profile `stage` copies into the release | `apply-root`'s rendering from the copy's profile must equal `stage`'s |
| The strategy timer's session start | `stage --session-start-utc` → `units/render.json` → the copy's `inputs.json` (checked `HH:MM[:SS]`) | `apply-root` renders from root-owned inputs only |
| The `current` link | A symlink renamed over the old one; on Windows (no symlink privilege) a file holding the id, read the same way | Windows rehearsals only |
| `CONVERGED`: the kernel recorded `RELEASE_BOUND` with this bundle | Read through the kernel heartbeat's `identity.bundle_digest` | The installer opens no ledger write path; the heartbeat carries the bound bundle |
| A started unit is verified by its handshake too | Its identity record only (unit `active`, own `MainPID`, bundle, revision, owner) | Each participant's handshake already refuses its own start (step 2) |
| The backup timer enabled when its destination is bound | Never enabled yet: there is no destination field until step 8 | Legacy's rule kept; step 8 adds the field |
| `--report` opened `O_NOFOLLOW | O_EXCL` relative to the work directory | Refused outside the work directory; the opening is step 6's | Smaller change; the path rule holds the boundary |
| Rehearsal units on Linux as transient user-manager units | `user_manager_argv` built and tested; `fake_systemctl` starts process groups on every platform | The rows that need the user-manager variant start production-profile processes: owner's word |
| PRF-CFG-06 for the strategy host and the observer | Kernel, market, supervisor and operator | Their `--check` needs SLICE-09's registry and step 5's observer section bound in the test profile |

**Not built, carried:** the supervisor's use of `read_mode` (`ledger_gap readonly` for `unavailable`, reading a crashed ledger through `copy`) and its break check "the supervisor opens `immutable` while the kernel runs" — not in part 1's seven items, so still open for step 7; the crash points CP-R2…CP-R7 as the configuration spec numbers them (three crash states and the staging crash are tested, not the full table); the Rollback (PRF-CFG-05: an older migration revision, an unread kind) and Composition (PRF-CFG-07) rows through the installer, which today rest on step 2's `compatible` and `validate_composition` tests.

**Not run: owner's word.** Every root row (real system users, the directory table on a host, the three store cases, kill release across users, `apply-root`, `enable-units`, `install-root-copy`, `pkcheck`, the real `sudo` rows, the two-uid case) is a row of step 10's staging; the production-profile rehearsal rows on the EC2 (the frozen kernel, the real `systemctl show`, `SIGTERM`, the network fence) wait for the owner's word.

**Left in OA_NEW, untracked:** the `build/` directory recorded in step 2.

## Step 8 — recovery set, backup, restore and the lease store, off production (outline)

Deepened only after the migration spec's SLICE-11 sections are written by their owner: the recovery-set manifest (A04-F5, `README.md:796`), the restore verification, the anchored heads, the send-lease store adapter (which every production kernel needs, step 2) and the clean-host rehearsal procedure. This step builds them against synthetic stores in temporary directories and a local stand-in for the off-host store, and composes `at-backup.service` from step 7's template. Test files are named by the migration spec (`tests/recovery/test_restore_fencing.py` exists in the tree).

## Step 9 — measurement on recorded sessions (outline)

Deepened after step 7 is built: the runtime spec's [measurement](../../design/runtime-isolation-observability-deployment.md#measurement-operationsverificationpy).

* **Pre-approved read-only work** (standing rules): copying recorded market days and legacy's records on the EC2 into an isolated directory, and reading the deployed OpenAlgo's settings with no order. Runs through `scripts/isolated_work.py` in `~/claude-desktop/` or the separate development checkout, never in the live checkout.
* **Produces** one `Measurement` per measured field in the register and in both specs' policy inputs, including the backup's reserve, SLICE-09's strategy-host waits and legacy's own read load on the account; the owner approves values with the release binding. Nothing is bound before that approval.
* **Feeds** PRF-ADM-07 and PRF-PRT-08 (their owners run them) and the PRF-PRT-10 baseline's recorded tick rates (SLICE-06 step 5).

## Step 10 — staging on the production host (outline; needs the owner's word)

Production-sensitive: "any deploy" is asked first under the standing rules. Deepened, then held until the owner says so for this release.

* **Does:** `deploy/install.py stage` and `check` for the exact candidate on the production host, ending at `VERIFIED_DISARMED`. No `activate`: nothing running is stopped or switched, and the legacy system keeps trading. Records the host's interpreter and systemd versions, `bundle.json`, and the account limits actually in force (the drop-in and any `limits.*` leaves of the deployed configuration, read without any credential file) for the owner to bind as `account.limits.*`.
* **Step 7's real-users rows** (the three store read cases, the strategy store, the market heartbeat, the operator CLI socket, cognition's socket, the channel, the two-uid case), if not run on a disposable host, run here on the owner's word, with the system users created by the owner's own sudo from the rendered `sysusers.d` file and the test units started as transient units; no unit file is written to `/etc/systemd/system` and nothing is enabled.
* **No supervisor run here.** Before SLICE-13's shadow, the target ledger on this host has no live obligation, so a supervisor assessment would prove nothing; PRF-OPS-09 runs with the shadow (SLICE-13).
* **Read budget.** `check` makes no broker call. Any broker read made in this step goes through the observation route and fits in what is left of the account's rate budget after legacy's own load, which step 9 measured.
* **Blocked until bound** (`check` refuses otherwise): `release.license`; the account settings; `recovery.lease_store` and the lease timings, which wait for the backup destination (POL-O01); every measured value step 9 produced.

## Step 11 — the real restore rehearsal (outline; needs the owner's word)

Production-sensitive: "the real restore rehearsal" is asked first under the standing rules, and it needs POL-O01 (the backup destination) answered. Deepened then from the migration spec's procedure: restore a real backup with the exact candidate artifact and schema on an isolated clean host; exercise crashes, unresolved effects, identity preservation, monotonic custody and refusal before fencing (D10, `README.md:1201`). The rehearsal reports its restore duration; it is judged against `recovery.rto_secs` only if the owner binds one (POL-047). Rehearsal evidence grants no activation.

## Completion conditions

* Every step's completion conditions hold, as written when it is deepened.
* PRF-CFG-01…08, PRF-CFG-10, PRF-CFG-11, PRF-OPS-01…07, PRF-PRT-14, PRF-PRT-06's frozen half and PRF-XC-02's production part pass; PRF-DOM-06 passes if step 5 was built.
* PRF-OPS-08's records exist and the owner has approved the values the release binds.
* Steps 10 and 11 are done on the owner's word, or recorded as waiting for it (with POL-O01 and the licence named). PRF-OPS-09 is carried to SLICE-13.
* Both specs and this file record each step "as built", with every Deviation and Fork call.
